PREFACE 
 
  I, the Chairman of the Department-related Parliam entary Standing Committee on Health and Family Welfare, having been 
authorized by the Committee to present the Report o n its behalf, do hereby present this Fifty-first Re port of the Committee on 
Action Taken by Government on the Recommendations c ontained in the Thirty-ninth Report on Demands for Grants (Demand 
No.46) of the Department of Health and Family Welfare, Ministry of Health and Family Welfare for the year 2010-2011. 
2.  The Thirty-ninth Report of the Department-relat ed Parliamentary Standing Committee on Health and Family Welfare was 
presented to Rajya Sabha and laid on the Table of L ok Sabha on 28th April, 2010. Replies of the Govern ment on the 
recommendations contained in the Report, received from the Department of Health and Family Welfare were considered by the 
Committee at its meeting held on the 27th January, 2011. 
3.   The Committee considered the draft Report and adopted the same at its meeting held on the 27th January, 2011. 
 
 
  BRAJESH PATHAK 
 
NEW DELHI ; Chairman,  
27 th 
 
January , 2011 Department-related Parliamentary  
Magha 7, 1932  (Saka)  Standing Committee on Health and Family Welfare.

CHAPTER-I
 
RECOMMENDATIONS/OBSERVATIONS IN RESPECT OF WHICH REPLIES OF THE GOVERNMENT HAVE 
BEEN ACCEPTED BY THE COMMITTEE  
II.  BUDGETARY ALLOCATION 
Recommendations/Observations  
2.10   Committee’s attention has also been drawn by  unspent balances lying with a number of Institutio ns. As on 1st 
December, 2009, maximum unspent balances amounting to Rs. 28.79 crore, Rs. 36.52 crore and Rs. 80.43 c rore were reported 
against the North-Eastern Indira Gandhi Regional Institute of Health and Medical Sciences, Shillong, LGB Regional Institute of 
Mental Health, Tezpur, Assam and AIIMS, New Delhi. Except AIIMS, the other two Institutes had unspent balances as on 1st 
April, 2009. Reasons for availability of such a hug e amount of unspent balances are not known to the C ommittee. However, 
such a state of affairs in national level institute s is indeed a cause  of concern for the Committee. This clearly shows that some 
major activities both ongoing/proposed might be adv ersely affected. The Committee would appreciate if the matter is taken up 
with the concerned authorities without further delay. 
Action Taken  
  The Department shares the concern of the Hon’ble Committee and has made it clear to the Institutions  concerned that 
further fund releases will depend on the pace of liquidation of their unspent balances. 
Recommendations/Observations  
2.13   The Committee notes that as a further effort  to maximize liquidation of UCs, a communication has been sent by the 
Secretary (Health) to the Principal Health Secretaries of all the States. If need be, this exercise ne eds to be pursued further with 
higher authorities. 
Action Taken  
  A D.O. letter at the level of Secretary (Health) is being issued to all the Chief Secretaries of the  States concerned after 
compilation of State-wise data pertaining to the grants released upto 31.03.2009. 
IV.   SAFDARJUNG  HOSPITAL  AND VARDHMAN MAHAVEER M EDICAL COLLEGE, NEW DELHI 
Recommendations/Observations  
4.2   The Committee notes that against projected Pl an requirement of Rs. 180.60 crore for 2010-11 for the Hospital, allocation 
of Rs. 118.60 crore has been made. Given the fact t hat out of the RE plan allocation of Rs. 129.92 cro re for 2009-10, the 
Hospital was able to spend Rs. 112.76 crore upto 28th February, 2010, with every possibility of full utilization by the end of the 
year, the Committee is unable to comprehend the rea sons behind a huge gap of Rs. 62.00 crore between t he projected and 
budgeted funds for 2010-11. The Committee, therefor e, recommends that additional funds be allocated fo r the Hospital at RE 
stage so that lack of funds should not hamper the re-developmental activities undertaken by the Hospital. 
Action Taken  
  Noted for compliance. 
Recommendations/Observations  
4.12   The Committee is constrained to observe that  the allocated funds for 2009-10 could not be utili zed even after steep 
reduction at the RE stage. To add to this the Depar tment’s reply that the reduction was due to realist ic assessment of the needs 
of the Institute, proves that the earlier estimate,  at the BE stage was not proper. Moreover, the fact  that the College could not 
utilize this reduced amount also makes it clear tha t there is some element of improper planning at som e stage. The Committee, 

accordingly, recommends to the Department to have a realistic assessment of the fund requirement for t he College at the outset 
itself. 
Action Taken  
  The suggestions of the Committee will be complied  with. 
VI.  ALL INDIA INSTITUTE OF MEDICAL SCIENCES (AIIMS ) 
Recommendations/Observations  
6.10  Committee’s attention has been drawn by a ver y disturbing trend of utilization of allocated fund s by AIIMS. Director, 
AIIMS during his deposition before the Committee, informed that during 2008-09 and 2009-10 some Plan Funds were diverted 
to Non-plan for paying the Sixth Pay commission’s a rrears and various other allowances to AIIMS employ ees. As a result, 
development projects could not be taken up. Frequen t changes in the plan projects also had a negative impact. The Committee 
would like to point out that diversion of plan fund s to non-plan side is in direct contravention of fi scal norms. The Committee 
deplores such a development at the premier Institute of the country. It seems that this trend has been  continuing since long. The 
Committee wonders about the role of Internal Audit Wing and Financial Adviser of the Department. The C ommittee is also 
very unhappy with the non-provision of required non-plan funds meant for payment of arrears and allowances to the employees 
which are anticipated beforehand. The Committee wou ld appreciate if fiscal management at AIIMS is brou ght to order. The 
Committee cannot accept the diversion of plan funds  meant for development projects to non-plan side. U rgent action needs to 
be, taken in this crucial matter so that this trend is not allowed to continue any further. 
Action Taken  
  The Institute was allocated Rs.566.00 crore (Non- Plan) and Rs.320.51 crore (Plan) against the requir ement of Rs.636.88 
crore (Non-Plan) and Rs.270.00 crore (Plan). Since there was requirement of Rs.636.88 crore (Non-Plan)  to meet the essential 
requirements of the Institute and to make payment o f arrear according to Sixth Pay Commission and fund  beyond Rs.566.00 
crore could not be provided by the Ministry under Non-Plan, Rs.70.00 crore were re-appropriated by the Ministry from the Plan 
fund. As desired by the Committee, every effort will be made to follow the fiscal discipline. 
VII.   POST GRADUATE INSTITUTE OF MEDICAL EDUCATION  AND RESEARCH (PGIMER),  
  CHANDIGARH  
Recommendations/Observations  
7.3  The Committee is happy to note that during the  first three years of the Eleventh Plan, there has not been a reduction in the 
Plan funds at the RE stage. This is an indication t hat the allocated funds are being fully utilized by  the Institute for its 
developmental activities. 
Action Taken  
  Efforts will continue to be made in this regard. 
X.   LADY HARDINGE MEDICAL COLLEGE AND SMT. SUCHETA  KRIPALANI HOSPITAL,  
  NEW DELHI 
Recommendations/Observations  
10.7  The Committee is very disturbed with the pers isting shortage of staff in the college/hospital un der the Department. The 
Committee recommends to the Department to make all conceivable efforts for an early filling up of the vacant posts so that the 
high standards of patient care are maintained. 
Action Taken  
  The shortage of staff is mainly in the category o f Safai Karmacharis (Group ‘D’ staff)/Clerks. To me et the shortage of 
Safai Karmacharis, the sanitation services are part ially outsourced. Since the appointments against th e Group ‘D’ posts cannot 
be made subsequent to the implementation of the 6th  Pay Commission recommendations, the proposal to pr ovide additional 

Safai Karmacharis by outsourcing is already under c onsideration in consultation with DGHS/the Institut ion concerned. As 
regards Clerks, the Institution has already taken up the matter with the Staff Selection Commission for filling up these posts. 
XIII. PRADHAN MANTRI SWASTHYA SURAKSHA YOJNA (PMSSY) 
Recommendations/Observations  
13.6   After an assessment of the progress of vario us components under the Scheme, the Committee in its 27th Report had 
made its apprehensions very clear that with the way  things were moving, it would take at least another  Plan period before the 
six AIIMS like Institutions would actually start fu nctioning. After a gap of two years, the Committee finds that its 
apprehensions have proved to be correct. It is stil l a distant dream to see these Institutions functio ning. The reason is that even 
after the completion of infrastructure related work , the Department has to concentrate on a whole lot of issues including 
manpower. The Committee would like to point out tha t this ambitious project is of critical importance for creating much 
required tertiary health care facilities in the cou ntry. Under funding of the project should not be al lowed to come in the way of 
access to quality and affordable health care for al l. The Committee, therefore, recommends that higher  level of funds, as 
required, be allocated under this head at RE stage during 2010-11.  
Action Taken  
  The concern of the Committee has been noted by th e Ministry. It is informed that in addition to the action taken as stated 
in Para 13.4, action for manpower planning and finalization of equipments for the six AIIMS-like institutions has been initiated. 
Manpower Committee constituted for finalization of manpower requirement has submitted its report and f urther follow-up 
action like identifying functions/duties, framing recruitment rules etc. is being taken. 
  Considering commencement of work at six AIIMS-lik e institutions, ongoing upgradation projects in the  first phase and 
likely start of second phase upgradation projects, Committee has rightly felt that the allocation of Rs.750 crore for 2010-11 will 
not be sufficient to meet the requirements of vario us activities under the PMSSY and therefore, the fu nd allocation should be 
enhanced to Rs.1500 crore. Steps will be taken at the appropriate time to propose enhanced outlay in RE 2010-11. 
Recommendations/Observations  
13.10   In the opinion of the Committee, the reason s cited for the delay are very flimsy in nature and  indicative of 
ineffectiveness of monitoring and coordination mech anism. The Committee is of the firm opinion that ha d proper coordination 
been done with the concerned agencies of the States  and if the Department had pursued the project vigo rously towards 
achieving the set targets, it could have approached  the State Governments at an appropriately higher l evel to thrash out State 
specific problems. The same is apparently lacking on the part of the Department. 
Action Taken  
  Noted. State Governments have constituted a Proje ct Monitoring Committee under the chairmanship of C hief Secretary, 
comprising of Secretary (Health/Medical Education),  representatives of local bodies/municipal authorit ies, civil works/forest 
departments, District Collector/Police Superintendent etc. in order to monitor the progress of work at  each of the AIIMS sites 
and also to resolve local/site specific issues. Regular meetings of the Committee are being held. 
Recommendations/Observations  
13.15  The Committee also recommends to the Departm ent to start ground work in respect of the issues l ike requirement of 
manpower, machines and equipments etc. so that once  the construction work is complete, no further dela y is caused in the 
operationalisation of the Institutes. 
Action Taken  
  Action for manpower planning for all the six AIIM S-like institutions has been initiated. Technical Committee constituted 
under the chairmanship of Director, PGIMER, Chandig arh worked out requirement of manpower to be recrui ted in phases. 
Ministry of Health and Family Welfare is in the pro cess of identifying functions and duties and also f raming of recruitment 
rules for each of the posts recommended by the Technical Committee. 
Recommendations/Observations  

13.16 The Committee takes a very serious view of th e persistent non-compliance of an assurance given b y the then Secretary 
during his presentation before the Committee during  examination of Demand for Grants (2008-09) of the Ministry to furnish a 
status report to the Committee regarding implementa tion of PMSSY project after every two months. The p roject is one of the 
landmark initiatives to enable the people of this c ountry across States to have access to quality heal thcare. Further, it involves 
enormous amount of precious tax payer’s money. Ther efore the Committee needs to keep a close watch on the progress of the 
Scheme. The Committee therefore reminds the Departm ent to be careful and keep its assurance given befo re a Parliamentary 
Committee mandated to scrutinize its work. 
Action Taken  
  Noted for compliance. A detailed Note in reply to  para 11.5 of the 27th Report of the Committee for 2008-09 was sent in 
March, 2010. A status report was also sent on 12.5.2010. 
XIV.  DEVELOPMENT OF NURSING SERVICES  
Recommendations/Observations  
14.5   The Committee is not happy with the abnormal  delays in bureaucratic procedures of seeking appro val. However, 
now that the new scheme has got the requisite appro val of the Cabinet, the Committee would recommend t o the Department to 
pursue the various components under the scheme vigo rously. The Committee also recommends to the Depart ment to make a 
realistic assessment of funds requirement for various components and seek more funds at the RE stage.  
Action Taken  
  Noted for compliance. As regards the realistic as sessment of funds, it is stated that the requiremen t is being worked out 
with the involvement of the concerned States and a total of Rs.17.70 crores has been released under va rious components of the 
scheme so far. Necessary funds would also be sought at the RE stage based on requirement. 
Recommendations/Observations  
14.7  The Committee is not prepared to accept the s tand of the Department that for both training and s trengthening of 
institutions proposals have to come from the States . When the Department has started the scheme, and r ightly so as per 
requirement of the profession, it has to take lead and responsibility to make it successful. It should  motivate the nursing 
institutions to come up with proposals to avail the  benefits under the scheme. Mere formal requests do  not make any scheme 
successful. Concerted efforts need to be made by the Department in this regard. 
Action Taken  
  Noted for compliance. Action is being taken. Conc erted efforts are being made for initiating the proposal from States. 
Recommendations/Observations  
14.14  The Committee is of the considered opinion t hat maintaining a Live Register of nurses would be of great help in 
formulating the various policies w. r. t.  the nursing personnel. Accordingly, the Department  needs to take up the issue with all 
the State Councils with some sense of urgency. The Department may also think of some kind of framework  whereby the State 
Councils are persuaded to start maintaining a live register of nurses as also share the details with t he Department to facilitate 
better policy formulation. 
Action Taken  
  Noted for compliance. 
Recommendations/Observations  
14.16  The Committee is very concerned with the sho cking findings of the nursing training programmes i n Bihar, Orissa, 
Rajasthan, Madhya Pradesh and Uttar Pradesh that ca me into light after the rapid assessment made by WH O, in collaboration 
with Indian Nursing Council. The picture of other States is also stated to be no better. While appreciating the initiative taken by 
the Department, the Committee recommends that a strategy needs to be chalked out whereby institutions getting grants from the 

Department are brought under some kind of regular t raining quality assessment schedule. This would not only help in plugging 
the loopholes but also help in devising better strategy for achieving desired training standards. 
 
Action Taken  
  Noted for compliance. As desired, a strategy will  be worked out. 
XVII. NATIONAL TOBACCO CONTROL PROGRAMME (NTCP)  
Recommendations/Observations  
17.7  The Committee is happy to note that the Depar tment is focusing on Mass Media/IEC campaign to gen erate awareness 
among the common people about the harmful effects of tobacco products. Awareness among the common masses, especially the 
younger generation, is the key to success of the wh ole Programme. The Committee would recommend that e vents like coming 
Commonwealth Games, venues of cricket and other mat ches being played in India, public gathering like t he kumbh melas etc. 
may also be utilized to generate awareness among people with Mass Media/ IEC campaign at the sites. 
Action Taken  
  Mass Media/IEC campaign through Prasar Bharti (Do ordarshan and All India Radio), NFDC and DAVP are b eing aired 
for creating awareness among the common people abou t the harmful effects of tobacco products. During t he Kumbh mela at 
Haridwar, special activities were undertaken for sensitizing about Tobacco Control in collaboration with NGOs. The Ministry is 
also developing “Guideline for Tobacco-free Commonwealth Games” in collaboration with Government of NCT of Delhi. 
XVIII. MEDICAL EDUCATION 
Recommendations/Observations  
18.4  The Committee is not inclined to agree with t he Department’s view that all is well with the exis ting procedure for 
recognition of MBBS degree colleges. The Department  cannot escape from its responsibility by citing th at it is the 
responsibility of the individual colleges to seek r ecognition. The Committee opines that the Departmen t needs to have into 
consideration the psychological impact of such a si tuation on the final year students who in its opini on would be more anxious 
to see their college getting recognition rather than taking their examinations seriously. The Committee is of the considered view 
that the whole procedure of recognition should be c omplete well before the final year students take th eir examinations. The 
Committee therefore recommends to the Department to  revisit its stand on the issue and come up with so me concrete steps. A 
status note on the issue of recognition to 15 medic al colleges where the first batches of MBBS student s have appeared for final 
examination in the academic year 2010 may be furnished. 
Action Taken  
  As per the provisions of MCI Regulations on Estab lishment of medical colleges, permission to establish a medical college 
and admit students is granted initially for a perio d of one year and is renewed on yearly basis subjec t to verification of 
achievements of annual targets. It is the responsibility of the person to apply to the MCI for purpose of renewal six months prior 
to the expiry of the initial permission. The MCI ca rries out inspections for annual renewal of permiss ion till such time the 
establishment of the Medical College and expansion of the hospital facilities are complete and a forma l recognition of the 
medical qualification of the college is granted. 
  The Committee’s view to complete whole procedure of recognition well before the final year student t ake their 
examination is appreciated. It will take some time to review the procedure of recognition as it requir es consultation with 
Medical Council of India. 
  Present status of 15 medical colleges where first  batch of MBBS students has appeared for final examination but are yet to 
be recognized in the academic year 2010 as on 14.5.2010 is at Annexure-XII . 
Recommendations/Observations  

18.7   The Committee is in full agreement with the initiatives proposed by the Dental Council of India (DCI) especially in 
view of the fact that so many seats are lying vacant year after year. Further, the Committee is of the firm opinion that education 
and accordingly the number of seats should be need based and keeping in view the demand and supply sce nario. Here, by the 
figures available with the Committee, it is amply clear that there is an urgent need for intervention to balance the same. In view 
of the above, the Committee recommends to the Depar tment to take some urgent steps in consultation wit h the DCI and the 
various State Governments, so that this unwanted trend is arrested at the earliest and precious resources are utilized purposefully 
in other areas of medical education. The Committee also recommends to the Department to actively consi der the proposal 
submitted to it by the DCI regarding making norms of opening new college more stringent. 
Action Taken  
  The DCI Regulations governing the establishment o f new dental colleges are made stringent and are be ing changed 
suitably viz . 
a.  Essentiality certificate from the State Governm ent with a statement that in case the applicant fai ls to create 
infrastructure for the dental college, fresh admiss ion will be stopped and the State Government shall take over the 
responsibility of the students already admitted in the college with the permission of the Central Government. 
b.  University affiliation from the concerned Unive rsity. 
c.  Attachment with own 100 bedded General Hospital / Government General Hospital/Medical College with a radius 
of 10 kms. 
d.  Accreditation of dental colleges by the externa l agency. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 

 
 
CHAPTER-II 
 
RECOMMENDATIONS/OBSERVATIONS WHICH THE COMMITTEE DOES NOT DESIRE TO PURSUE IN 
VIEW OF THE GOVERNMENT’S REPLIES 
II   BUDGETARY  ALLOCATION  
Recommendations/Observations  
2.4   With this trend of allocation of funds and ut ilization thereof, the Committee does not have much  hope about optimum 
release of funds out of the approved outlay of Rs. 41092.92 crore in the remaining two years of the El eventh Plan. The 
Department will have to make concerted efforts so a s to ensure optimum utilization of plan allocation of Rs. 5560.00 crore in 
2010-11. Only then, there are chances of enhanced provision being made in 2011-12, the last year of the Eleventh Plan.  
Action Taken  
  The Department notes the concerns of the Committe e and submits that major hurdles like poor absorpti ve capacity under 
the NRHM and lack of approval of many major schemes  having been removed, there are very good prospects  of larger fund 
utilization during the last two years of the Plan. 
Recommendations/Observations  
2.5   The Committee in its 27th Report on Demands f or Grants (2008-09) had commented on the Department ’s inability to 
project its budgetary requirements in an effective way for the simple reason that the utilization of f unds during the Tenth Plan 
was far from satisfactory. The Committee finds that  the same trend continues in the Eleventh Plan peri od also. During 2007-08 
and 2009-10, keeping in view the pace of expenditure, allocated funds were substantially reduced at the RE stage. What is more 
worrisome is that the Department could not achieve optimum utilization of this reduced allocation duri ng these two years. 
Enhanced Plan allocation of Rs. 4450.00 in 2009-10 failed to serve the purpose as it had to be brought  down to Rs. 3825.25 
crore, marginally higher than the 2008-09 allocation of Rs. 3650.00 crore. Utilization status in 2009-10 was more disappointing. 
As on 28th February, 2010, expenditure reported was Rs. 2559.61 crore which is less than the utilization of Rs. 3008.40 crore in 
the previous year. The Committee also takes note of the fact that expenditure reported upto 31st January, 2010 under Plan head 
was only Rs. 2034.06 crore. This clearly points to the fact that major utilization is taking place in the last quarter of the financial 
year, thus indicating non-adherence to fiscal discipline.  
Action Taken  
  As per the guidelines issued by the Ministry of F inance, monthly expenditure plan is drawn up each year and is appended 
to the Detailed Demand for Grants. Sincere efforts are made to adhere to this fiscal discipline. Howev er, sometimes procedural 
delays in obtaining necessary approvals for incurring the expenditure leads to major utilization of funds during the last quarter.

Recommendations/Observations  
2.9   The Committee is specially concerned with the  slow pace of implementation of schemes like Pradha n Mantri Swasthya 
Suraksha Yojana (PMSSY), Cancer Research, National Mental Health Programme, Assistance for Capacity Bu ilding for 
Trauma Centers, Oversight Committee, Upgradation/st rengthening of Nursing Services, Strengthening/Upgr adation of 
Pharmacy schools/colleges, Strengthening/creation of paramedical institutions as well as underutilizat ion of allocated funds by 
some of the Hospitals/institutions. The Committee o bserves that this recurrent and persistent trend of  underutilization is 
indicative of ineffectiveness of monitoring of impl ementation of schemes and utilization of funds. The  Committee therefore 
recommends to the department to keep the activities  of these schemes/institutions under constant scrut iny/monitoring and take 
urgent remedial steps so that the stumbling blocks in the optimum utilization of allocated/sanctioned funds are removed. 
Action Taken  
  The major bottlenecks in the implementation of th e major components of PMSSY were non-finalisation o f tenders for 
construction of hospital and medical colleges for A IIMS-type institutions. This process has been compl eted and the Scheme is 
due to gather momentum. Similarly, major approvals under Oversight Committee pertaining to PGIMER, Cha ndigarh and 
AIIMS have been finalised/ are nearing finalization . In the light of the concerns of the Committee, th e Health Secretary has 
carried out a special review of the Schemes for Development of Trauma Centres, Nursing Services and Development of Human 
Resources. It has been decided to assist the States to prepare suitable proposals under these Schemes. 
Recommendations/Observations  
2.12   The Committee takes note of the fact that th e Department has taken remedial measures like for n on-recurring 
grants, no fresh releases are being authorized unless audited Utilization Certificate for grant released in the year prior to the last 
year ( i.e.  with a ‘grace period’ of one year) has been received, subject to the condition that the sanction for the earlier grant does 
not permit a longer period of utilization. Further,  the provisions of the General Financial Rules, whi ch require that in case of 
recurring grants, funds for the same purpose beyond  75% of the next year’s provision will not be relea sed unless audited 
Utilization Certificate for the grants released in the previous year is received, are being strictly i mplemented. Impact of such 

measures does not seem to be very effective as numb er of pending UCs continues to remain very high. No t only this, such a 
trend also indicates that schemes are not being implemented as envisaged. 
 
Action Taken  
  Instructions are being issued to all Divisional h eads from time-to-time to review the position of ou tstanding UCs and to 
maximize their efforts in liquidation of pending UC s. A D.O. has also been issued at the level of Secr etary (Health) to all 
Principal Health Secretaries. Recently an O.M. has been issued at the level of AS & FA on 14-05-2010 t o all the Joint 
Secretaries vide  which the need for settlement of UCs in respect of  grants released upto 31-03-2009 in terms of GFR, 2 005 has 
been emphasized.

Department has to justify the need for additional f unds by ensuring optimum utilization thereof and be  careful in 
placing/proposing its revised estimate so that funds are not unnecessarily blocked and go unspent.

improvement is expected during the last two years of the current Plan. The details of utilisation of budget allocation, under Plan,

Recommendations/Observations  
3.5   The Committee does not find any logic for und er provisioning for CGHS under Materials and Suppli es as also for IT 
activities Head which are bound to affect the quali ty of services available to the CGHS beneficiaries.  The Committee also 
observes that total tentative pendency with regard to medical claims of pensioners as on January, 2010  is Rs. 200 crore, 
including bills pertaining to 2008-09. In such a sc enario, allocation of Rs. 377.87 crore for pensione rs against the projected 
requirement of Rs. 627.00 crore for 2010-11 is boun d to increase the pendency further. The Committee i s of the view that full 
allocation of required funds has to be there so that pensioners are not made to face uncalled for hardships. 
Action Taken  

  Budget head “PORB” meant for debiting of bills of  hospitals and diagnostic centres in connection with treatment given to 
pensioner CGHS beneficiaries now stands transferred  to the demands for grants of the Ministry of Healt h and Family Welfare 
from the Ministry of Finance. It is, therefore, pos sible, at short notice, for the Ministry of Health and Family Welfare to re-
appropriate funds from other heads of accounts with in the budget allocation of the Ministry, which was  not possible earlier 
when the funds were allotted by the Ministry of Finance. 
  Requirement of funds for “PORB” head of account i s being reviewed from time to time and additional allocation of funds 
under this head would be arranged by re-appropriati on from other heads of accounts with the concurrenc e of Internal Finance 
Division. 
Recommendations/Observations  
3.12  The process of computerization under the CGHS which was initiated during 2005 is continuing. The Committee notes that 
finally the Department has been able to finish the work of computerization in six cities i.e.  Delhi, Kolkata, Nagpur, Pune, 
Chennai and Ranchi and it is nearing completion in Hyderabad, Mumbai and Patna. However, the Committee  is constrained to 
note that the Department has not been able to add m omentum to the computerization work even after the expiry of about five 
years as 15 cities still remain to be covered. The Committee therefore recommends that the computeriza tion work in the 
remaining cities be completed in a mission mode by the end of the Eleventh Plan.  
Action Taken  
  The computerisation of functioning of CGHS in all  cities, in so far as allopathy stream is concerned, has been completed. 
Computerisation process of AYUSH stream has been st arted in Delhi and will be gradually extended to ot her cities as the 
software required for operating AYUSH wellness centres is different from the one required for allopathy stream of medicines. 
Recommendations/Observations  
3.14   The Committee appreciates the steps taken by  the Department to neutralize the teething problems  afflicting the 
smooth implementation of the computerization. Howev er, the ground realities are not much encouraging. The Committee is 
informed that there is shortage of computer operato rs and the doctors have to first see the patients a nd then feed the relevant 
data in the computer system leading to delays and the resultant long queues. This way the computerization has only been able to 
help keep a check on the inventory in the store roo m and save time in putting up request for local pur chases wherever required. 
However, it has not been able to obviate the problems faced by the beneficiaries particularly the old age pensioners who have to 
stand for long hours in queue. The Committee, there fore, recommends that adequate trained manpower may  be made available 
at the earliest. 
Action Taken  
  The services of data entry operators have been ma de available in wellness centres till such time the  doctors acquaint 
themselves of the computerised functioning of the C GHS. It needs to be appreciated that doctors in dev eloped countries and in 
major hospitals in India themselves operate computer while attending to patients. At the click of the mouse the doctors are able 
to see the entire medical history of the patient. D octors will necessarily have to look at the monitors for finding out the medical 
history of the patient if the data entry were to be done only by data entry operators. This will only delay the diagnosis process. It 
is only the doctor who would know the module for th e prescription of medicines, which a data entry ope rator would have no 
knowledge of. The general feedback received from th e doctors is that though they were initially appreh ensive about working / 
operating on a computer, now they feel confident and easy to handle and are comfortable with the system. 
Recommendations/Observations  
3.16   The Committee is happy to note that after mu ch delay the Department has finally been able to op en a dispensary 
each in NOIDA and Dwarka. However, as the number of  CGHS beneficiaries is very large in that area, the  Committee 
recommends to the Department to put all its acts to gether to start the three dispensaries for which pl ots have been purchased in 
Dwarka and one dispensary at Gurgaon. 

Action Taken  
  Dwarka:  Recently another wellness centre has been sanction ed for Sector 23, Dwarka, for which a plot of land is 
available. However, till such time the construction  at the said plot is started/completed, an advertis ement was released in 
newspapers for hiring suitable accommodation there.  Against this advertisement an offer of an accommod ation has been 
received at Dwarka at Sector 14, 15. CGHS is actively pursuing the matter for hiring the accommodation there. 
  Gurgaon:  Central Design Bureau, Ministry of Health and Family Welfare, has prepared the plan for .construction on plot 
in possession of the CGHS at Sector 5. CPWD has sub mitted an estimate. Funds to the tune of Rs.5,62,61 ,000/- have been 
sanctioned during financial year 2010-11. These fun ds are being placed at disposal of CPWD under Minis try of Urban 
Development for the construction. 
Recommendations/Observations  
3.21   The Committee is not very happy with the ele ment of adhocism in such a crucial area. The Committee would like to 
point out that the number of vacancies in a particu lar grade are bound to put pressure on the existing  workforce thereby 
compromising with the quality of services provided to the beneficiaries. The Committee, therefore, rec ommends to the 
Department to urgently chalk out a strategy to fill up the vacant positions in all grades under CGHS in a time-bound manner. 
Action Taken  
  In General Duty Medical Officers (GMDO) sub-cadre  of CHS, against the sanctioned strength of 667 in CGHS, Delhi, 
606 have been filled up (including 45 retired GDMOs , who have been appointed on contract basis). Again st the 61 vacancies, 
45 candidates from Combined Medical Service Examination (CMSE) 2008 were proposed for posting (issue of offer) in CGHS, 
Delhi, out of which 24 candidates, whose pre-appoin tment formalities have been completed, have been is sued offer of 
appointment in CGHS, Delhi and 4 posting / transfer  orders are in standby. For filling up of the remai ning vacancies and those 
posts against which retired GDMOs are working on co ntractual basis, offers of appointment will be issu ed from the candidates 
of forthcoming CMSE 2009. 
  Posts at SAG level, with the extension of Dynamic  Assured Career Progression Scheme (DACP) from CMO (NFSG) to 
SAG level in GMDO sub-cadre, which grants time-boun d promotion irrespective of vacancy, all vacant pos ts at SAG level all 
over India would be filled up by promotion of CMO ( NFSG) officers. The proposal for promotion is at an  advanced stage of 
consideration. 
  Requisition for filling up of vacancies of 45 Spe cialists, including 24 in CGHS of non-teaching sub- cadre have been sent 
to the UPSC. The matter is being pursued with the UPSC. 
Recommendations/Observations  
3.23   The Committee has noticed substantial variat ion in approved rates of investigations / treatment  procedures in various 
metro cities. The Committee does not find much meri t in the argument of the Department that rates offe red by the private 
hospitals and diagnostic centres depend upon the pr evailing market rates, number and business volume f rom CGHS 
beneficiaries likely to be derived by eligible inst itutions/private institutions, which vary from city  to city. The Committee is of 
the opinion that substantial variation of 50 to 100  per cent in approved rates and that too in cities like Delhi, Mumbai, Chennai 
and Kolkata cannot be considered commensurate with the variation in economic dynamics. The Committee a ccordingly, 
recommends to the Department to come up with a stra tegy to counter this variation of rates by having m inimum disparities in 
the rates.  
Action Taken  
  CGHS has initiated the process for revision of ra tes and for fresh empanelment of hospitals and diag nostic centres in all 
the cities in which it is operating. It has been de cided that since CGHS Delhi accounts for roughly ha lf of the beneficiary base 
of CGHS, for purposes of arriving at rates for different procedures/ tests/imaging services, etc., there will be an upper cap on the 
rates, in that the rates in cities other than Delhi cannot have rates higher than that in Delhi. 

  The above process was adopted in the empanelment procedure initiated during 2004-07, which resulted in hospitals in 
Mumbai not accepting the rates and refusing to acce pt CGHS beneficiaries. Instructions had, therefore,  to be issued to, permit 
CGHS beneficiaries to take treatment in any hospital and claim reimbursement as per 2001-02 rates. 
  Pensioner beneficiaries in Mumbai sought relief f rom the High Court of Mumbai, against which the Hig h Court has 
ordered that the reimbursement be made to beneficia ries after adding certain percentage to the higher of either 2001 or 2007 
rates, pending finalisation of the process of revision of rates initiated by the CGHS. 
  No hospital participated in the tender floated by  CGHS, Mumbai for revision of rates and for fresh e mpanelment of 
private hospitals. CGHS, Mumbai has issued advertis ement for re-tendering the process of fresh empanel ment and revision of 
package rates for CGHS, Delhi. The High Court of Mumbai has directed hospitals to participate in the tender process. 
Recommendations/Observations  
3.25   Though the Committee appreciates Department’ s initiative for ensuring quality service to CGHS b eneficiaries and 
has no doubts over the professionalism of NABH/NABL , yet it does not find much merit in making it comp ulsory as a 
precondition for empanelment under CGHS. The NABL/N ABH lay down certain norms and standards to be obse rved and on 
inspection, they provide accreditation to those who  fulfil the said standards. There is no guarantee t hat once accredited, the 
Hospital/diagnostic centre will maintain those standards till the next inspection by NABL/NABH. Further, it has been observed 
that many of the reputed hospitals don’t go for acc reditation under them. In such a scenario it may be  counterproductive as the 
beneficiaries may be deprived of services under suc h reputed Hospitals/diagnostic centres. The Committ ee accordingly 
recommends to the Department to be cautious on this move. 
3.26   The Committee would also like to have inform ation regarding the number of NABH/NABL accredited Government 
hospitals/diagnostic centres as well as private hospitals/diagnostic centres empanelled under CGHS. 
Action Taken  
  When the CGHS initiated and completed the process  of empanelment of private hospitals and diagnostic  centres during 
2004, the so called big hospitals acted pricey and did not accept the rates arising out of tender proc ess. CGHS was left with no 
option but to empanel only those hospitals and diag nostic centres which accepted the package rates. CG HS has no power to 
compulsorily empanel hospitals and diagnostic centr es under it. After the completion of empanelment pr ocess, considerable 
doubts were expressed in various forums that CGHS e mpanelled “fly-by night operators” and “big hospita ls have been 
eliminated from the tendering process”. One point that no one appreciated was that CGHS had prescribed the parameters for the 
hospitals and diagnostic centres to fulfil before e mpanelment and also that the empanelment orders wer e not issued without the 
specific recommendation of the Quality Council of I ndia. Quality Council of India (QCI) is an organisa tion set up by the 
Ministry of Commerce and Industry. A team of QCI wo uld physically inspect the premises of the hospital s and diagnostic 
centres to confirm that they conformed to the param eters fixed by the CGHS, and only those hospitals a nd diagnostic centres 
were recommended to the CGHS for empanelment as wer e fulfilling the prescribed conditions. In addition , the hospitals and 
diagnostic centres were required to submit bank guarantees and sign MOA with CGHS, which had stringent conditions. 
  The criticism of CGHS of ignoring “big” hospitals  continues even today though on a slightly muted condition. 
  According to QCI, accreditation is a process base d on a system implemented across the organisation. It has to be practiced 
by its management, staff, clients (patients) all the time and being continuously monitored, leaving no room for relaxation of the 
requirements. Moreover, it is a process adopted by the management with an aim to improve its services.  It is different from an 
inspection which is based on the verification of in frastructure on fixed time basis. Therefore, accred itation mechanism is 
entirely different from the inspection. Accreditati on body also monitors the performance of .its accre dited facilities on a 
continuous basis by way of on-site surveillance ass essments, renewal assessments, off-site monitoring using data on various 
indicators, verifying complaints from users of the services, etc. Thus, a hospital once accredited doe s not mean that it will 
remain accredited forever. NABH can suspend or with draw accreditation on various grounds laid down in its policies and 
known to accredited hospitals. Patient safety and qualify of care are cardinal points in the accreditation. The accredited hospital 
needs to establish systems, processes and more impo rtantly monitor key clinical outcomes. All major gr oups of hospital 
industry, i.e.  Apollo, Fortis, Max Wockhardt, Columbia Asia, Narayana Hrudalaya, etc., have adopted NABH accreditation. 

  Unlike regulation, accreditation is voluntary. Ac creditation is driven by market forces, empanelling  and paying agencies. 
It stands to logic that paying agency has the right  to demand quality and the CGHS has taken the appro priate decision to 
demand accreditation so as to provide safe healthcare to its beneficiaries. 
  Presently, 45 private and 2 Government hospitals have been accredited by NABH and there are over 400  applications for 
accreditation which are under different stages of evaluation for accreditation. Over 70 hospitals have undergone pre-assessment 
and now working towards final assessment. Over 20 h ospitals have undergone final assessment and now wo rking to close the 
deficiencies so that case can be put to the Accredi tation Committee for recommendation. It is a dynami c process and the 
statistics keep changing as hospitals keep moving from one step to another. 
  It is learnt that after the CGHS imposed the cond ition of accreditation with NABH / NABL for empanel ment under it, 
QCI received over 300 applications for accreditation, which proves that private hospitals and diagnostic centres need CGHS for 
their profitability as much as CGHS wants them to s erve its beneficiaries. The condition will, in the long run, prove beneficial 
to the welfare of CGHS beneficiaries, which is a win-win situation for CGHS.

requirements of the Institute for the current Plan period leading to excess allocation of funds. It is  highly impractical to make 
out that the Institute can spend an enormous amount  of Rs. 330 crore left for the final year of the Pl an. The Committee would 
like to be updated in this regard.

only exception is the Advanced Trauma Centre (Phase -I) which is stated to be complete but not function al. The Committee

VIII. NORTH EASTERN INDIRA GANDHI REGIONAL INSTITUT E OF HEALTH AND MEDICAL  
  SCIENCES (NEIGRIHMS), SHILLONG  
Recommendations/Observations  
8.3   The Committee would like to be briefed about the components of ‘further development plan’ of the  Institute. The 
Committee would also like to be given an idea wheth er the Institute has become fully operational and a ll the pending items of 
developmental work have been completed. 
Action Taken  
  The Institute, as part of further development pla n, is finalizing the proposals of MBBS hostel, Regi onal Cancer Centre 
and Nursing College. 
  The Institute has also been given in-principle ap proval by the Ministry of Health & Family Welfare for courses viz . DM in 
Cardiology, MS in General Surgery and MD in General  Medicine and increase MD/MS seats in the Departmen ts of Pathology, 
Microbiology, Anaesthesia subject to recruitment of additional faculty. 
  The Institute is fully functional with 22 Departm ents (inclusive of 4 super specialty departments - Urology, Cardiology, 
CTVS and Neurology). The Institute is having fully operational Blood Bank with facility for blood separation. 
  The procurement of equipment has been made for al l departments which are presently functional. 

  The Diagnostic Departments like Radiology, Bioche mistry, Physiology, etc. are fully equipped. A well equipped Casualty 
(Medicine) has also been made operational. 
  The Institute was formally inaugurated on 5th Mar ch, 2010. 
 
 
Recommendations/Observations  
8.6   The Committee is very concerned at the contin uance of a very large number of vacant positions in  the Institute that is 
supposed to be developed on the pattern of AIIMS, New Delhi and PGIMER, Chandigarh and provide specialized health care to 
the deprived regions of North East India. The Commi ttee notes that the efforts of the Institute to fil l up Group “B” and “C” 
technical posts have yielded some fruits. But the s ame is not the case w. r. t.  other categories of posts. The Committee wonders 
as to how the Institute can be considered functiona l when more than one-third of sanctioned Faculty po sts are lying vacant and 
out of total strength of 189 posts of SRD/JRD, only  83 are in position. Both the teaching and healthca re facilities as envisaged 
are simply not there at present in the Institute. T he Committee is of the opinion that manpower alongw ith the infrastructure is 
the foremost important ingredient which is a must for smooth functioning of this Institute. The infrastructure having been put in 
place to a satisfactory level needs to be backed wi th requisite manpower. The Committee accordingly re commends to the 
Department to approach the CAT (Guwahati Bench) or an appropriate authority with a sense of urgency in  public interest so 
that the case is settled or at least the stay is va cated to facilitate an early filling up of the vaca ncies. The Department needs to 
keep its relentless efforts to attract manpower by special incentives and special recruitment drives. The Committee may be 
updated on the issue.  
Action Taken  
  Due to the stay order of CAT (Guwahati Bench), th e Institute is not able to appoint faculty. Efforts  are being made to 
appoint faculty on ad-hoc  basis. The Standing Legal Counsel of the Institute  has informed that the appointment of faculty on 
ad-hoc  basis does not violate CAT order. Special efforts have been made to fill up the vacant faculty posts. 
  Vide Government order dated 31.12.2009 and 12.01. 2010 NEIGRIHMS’ Faculty has been put at par with fa culty of 
AIIMS, New Delhi, PGIMER, Chandigarh, JIPMER, Puddu cherry, NIMHANS, Bangalore with regard to pay and s ervice 
conditions. 
  The faculty of the Institute has been paid specia l duty allowance and provided fully furnished accommodation; 2 seats for 
MBBS course from Meghalaya quota have been reserved for wards of the faculty.

in a national level premier institute, especially because of High Court intervention. The Department must be aware that the area 
of services where CIP used to hold a commanding pos ition in the earlier times is the most neglected ar ea of our present-day 
healthcare.  
Action Taken  
  The process is being expedited.

  NEW DELHI.  

Recommendations/Observations  
10.4   The Committee is disappointed that there has  been considerable delay in achieving the target of  first phase of 
redevelopment of the college and its associated hos pital. When enquired about the same, the Principal and Medical 
Superintendent of the College during his deposition  before the Committee on 23rd March, 2010 informed that EFC for the 
comprehensive redevelopment plan envisaged three ye ars back has already been cleared. The same was app roved by the 
Cabinet Committee of Economic Affairs in the beginn ing of the last year. The approved plans were subse quently submitted to 
specific authorities of the Delhi Government and ot her institutions and out of 12 such authorities, ap proval from the Delhi 
Urban Arts Commission and the Forest Department was yet to be received. Accordingly, construction work could not be started. 
As soon as all the NOCs are available, tender would  be floated for construction of buildings under pha se -1 of Comprehensive 
Redevelopment Plan.  
Recommendations/Observations  
10.5   The Committee would appreciate if all the pr ocedural delays coming in the way of Phase-I of Com prehensive 
Redevelopment Plan of the Lady Hardinge Medical Col lege and Sucheta Kriplani Hospital are removed at t he earliest. The 
Department needs to play a more pro-active role in such matters. 
Action Taken  
1. Immediately after the Architectural Consultant M /s G. D. Sambhare & Co. submitted the redevelopment plan to the 
Local Authorities for clearance, the matter was tak en up by the Ministry of Health & Family Welfare wi th these 
authorities to facilitate early clearance of the LH MC plan. As a result, all the clearances except fro m the Forest 
Department and Delhi Urban Arts Commission (DUAC) h ave been received by the Institution. As regards DU AC, 
it has found the concept of the proposal acceptable  subject to certain observations. After incorporati ng changes 
accordingly, the revised plan has been submitted to  NDMC for referring the plan to DUAC for final appr oval. As 
soon as the DUAC gives its final approval, the plan  shall be submitted to the Forest Department for ne cessary 
clearance. The Notice Inviting Tenders (NIT) can be issued only after the necessary clearances are received from all 
the authorities. 
2.  To avoid any delay in issuing the NITs for the project after the necessary clearances are obtained , the Draft NITs 
were referred to the Ministry of Law & Justice (Department of Legal Affairs) for approval. They have made certain 
observations. The Draft NITs are under revision as per the observations of the Department of Legal Aff airs. Thus, 
by the time clearances are obtained, the Draft NITs  shall be ready. This would circumvent the possibil ity of any 
delay in issuing the NITs after the necessary clearances are received.

XIII. PRADHAN MANTRI SWASTHYA SURAKSHA YOJNA (PMSSY) 
Recommendations/Observations  
13.4   Such a slow progress of implementation is bo und to result in huge amount remaining unutilized a t the end of the 
Plan period. The Committee had time and again been inviting the attention of the Department towards th e urgent need to take 
some corrective measures, but it appears that the same has not yielded fruits atleast as of now. 
Action Taken  
  Progress has been slow in the past. However, all efforts are being taken now to implement the PMSSY Projects quickly. 
Civil work for package I, i.e. construction of medi cal college complex has been awarded and the work h as started. Civil work 
for package-II, i.e. hospital complex has been re-tendered. Upgradation of Trivendrum Medical College has been completed and 
work at other medical colleges in the first phase o f PMSSY is progressing. Upgradation programme in the second phase is also 
gathering momentum. In order to close monitoring/ i mplementation of PMSSY projects, a retired Addition al Director General 
of CPWD has been appointed as Project Advisor in Project Monitoring Unit of PMSSY. 
Recommendations/Observations  
13.12  The Committee has been monitoring the progre ss of implementation of PMSSY very closely. The Com mittee observes 
that scheduled dates of various stages of different  components of both AIIMS like institutions alongwi th their residential 
complexes and upgradation of 13 existing medical co lleges have been revised time and again for varying  reasons. The 
Committee is perturbed to note that this ambitious programme was launched on 15th July, 2003. Even after a lapse of more than 
six years, the likely date for selection of contrac tors has been given as April, 2010, with civil work  supposed to start in June, 
2010 and date of completion projected as December, 2012. As per the latest information, bids for the p roject are under 
finalization. Similarly, with regard to residential  complexes, time-lines have been revised, indicatin g a delay of six months to 
one year. The Committee apprehends that there are a ll chances of further revision of date of completio n. For example, whereas 
only 15 percent and 20 percent of construction work  in respect of Bhubaneswar and Bhopal residential c omplex, respectively, 
has been reported, their likely date of completion is December, 2010.  
Action Taken  
  Slow progress in Bhopal site is due to certain si te specific issues such as removal of encroachers and shifting of overhead 
high tension wire and delay in obtaining building p ermission from State Government. Similarly, housing  project at 
Bhubaneswar has been delayed due to non-availability of a portion of land which is presently under lit igation. Therefore, scope 

of work for housing complex has been reduced from 1 8 to 9 blocks and contract price has been revised t o Rs.54.75 crore. 63% 
of housing work at Rishikesh and 58% at Patna have been completed. The work at Jodhpur and Raipur sites was completed. 
  For Package-I civil work, i.e. medical college co mplex, work was awarded in April, 2010. Civil work for Package-II has 
also been recently awarded in April, 2010. Civil work for Package-II has also been recently awarded. 
Recommendations/Observations  
13.13   Position with regard to upgradation of 13 m edical colleges is also quite discouraging. Here also with the exception 
of Trivandrum Medical College (reported to be complete and ready for inauguration), likely date of completion in respect of the 
remaining colleges indicates shifting 6 months to one year. Here again in respect of RIMS, Ranchi 15 p ercent progress, and for 
Srinagar Medical College, 39 percent progress has been made so for. 
Action Taken  
  The civil work at RIMS, Ranchi could only be star ted in September, 2009 due to failure of earlier bids and subsequent re-
tendering. There are also site specific issues such  as shifting of overhead electrical line passing ov er the proposed Oncology 
Block. 
  As regards Srinagar Medical College, climatic con ditions as well as periodical local disturbances in  the region caused 
slow progress in civil work.  
Recommendations/Observations  
13.14  The Committee appreciates the candid admissi on of the Secretary regarding errors of judgment of  the Department and 
lack of adequate monitoring while implementing the Scheme. The Committee would, however, like to point  out that this alone 
would not suffice. The new monitoring mechanism has to be in place immediately and every effort has to  be made to adhere to 
the fresh timelines set under various components of  the Scheme. The Committee would also like to empha size that the revised 
estimate of  
Rs. 820 crore for each AIIMS like Institution is more than two and half times when compared with origi nal estimate of Rs. 332 
crore. Therefore, it is essential to adhere to the new time lines, otherwise there is every possibilit y of further escalation. The 
Committee finds that PMSSY was conceptualized in the first year of the Tenth Plan. As the things stand today, one can hope of 
its becoming operational only in the beginning of the Twelfth Plan.  
Action Taken  
  Project Cell comprising of Superintending Enginee r and Financial Adviser have already been put in pl ace at each of the 
sites. In addition, a retired Additional Director G eneral of CPWD has been appointed as Project Adviso r in Project Monitoring 
Unit of PMSSY. 
  A society at each site is also being formed in or der to facilitate in greater autonomy and faster execution of projects. 
XIV.  DEVELOPMENT OF NURSING SERVICES 
Recommendations/Observations  
14.6  On an analysis of various physical targets se t and their achievement level during the first three years of the Eleventh Plan, 
the Committee finds a discouraging scenario. Achiev ement in the most crucial component, i.e., training  of nursing personnel 
has remained much below the targeted coverage. Against a target of training of 8000, 9000 and 12000 nursing personnel during 
2007-08, 2008-09 and 2009-10, funds could be releas ed for training of only 2400, 2880 and 2800 nursing  personnel during 
these three years. It is not known as to whether the reported number of nurses could be eventually trained.  
Action Taken  
  The targets are for the period when the scheme wa s in the process of revision and therefore could no t be up to the mark. 
Since the CCEA has approved the modified scheme, all out efforts are being made to implement the scheme earnestly.

minimal. Against an allocation of Rs. 70.00 crore i n both the years, expenditure reported at the end o f year was only Rs. 14.57 
crore and Rs. 23.26 crore, respectively. Plan alloc ation of Rs. 70.00 crore in 2009-10 was reduced to Rs. 55.00 crore at the RE 
stage. The expenditure reported as on 28th February 2010 is Rs. 38 crore only.  
 
 
Action Taken  
  There has been delay in initiating expenditure du ring the first two quarters of 2009-10 because CCEA  approval for 
Manpower Components (1st EFC) of NMHP was obtained in March, 2009 the sanction of which could be conve yed to the 
states only in the end of April 2009 due to model c ode of conduct for general election being in force.  Due to this completed 
proposals were received from the State Government i n subsequent months and after due inspection of the  institutes short listed 
for centers of excellence in mental health recommendations for funding could be completed by August 2009. However, later on 
by the end of the 4th quarter, an amount of Rs.51.5 crore was spent.

Action Taken  
  At the end of 2009-10 the achievement level for t he last year stands revised to initiation of 7 centers of excellence and 19 
PG training departments in mental health at 9 insti tutes in addition to support for 2 old DMHPs and 3 Psychiatry wings. Other 
proposed schemes such as new DMHPs, NGO/PPP scheme,  Monitoring & Evaluation, Research & Training has n ot yet been 
approved. Due to lack of dedicated monitoring mecha nism in the programme the monitoring of existing DM HPs the utilisation 
of funds have been rather slow and procurement of c omplete proposals under various schemes of NMHP was  adversely 
affected. The monitoring and evaluation component has now been approved by the competent authority before initiating it. Lack 
of qualified professionals for the programme which was affecting the programme adversely in States is being addressed by 
implementing manpower development schemes and provi sion of using the trained manpower in DMHP team til l the time 
qualified manpower is available. With a dedicated m onitoring mechanism, production of more trained man power in mental 
health, the provision of using trained DMHP team in the absence of qualified mental health professionals, the implementation of 
the programme is expected to improve. 

Recommendations/Observations  
15.8  The Committee is aware that active cooperatio n of the State Governments is key to the success of  a Programme of vast 
magnitude like this. However, it cannot buy the arg ument that it is only the States who are responsibl e for not achieving the 
intended results under the Programme. The fact that  EFC considered the proposal of NMHP only in July, 2008 and only after 
that it was placed before the CCEA for approval is proof of the procedural hassles that are hampering the Programme. Further, 
the Department needs to take a pro-active approach and try to bring all the States on board for effect ive implementation of the 
Programme and have regular interactions with them. It is high time that the problem areas hampering the implementation of the 
Programme are identified and suitable and timely action taken accordingly. 
Action Taken  
  Lack of a dedicated cell/manpower at the Central level is an area of concern. This was reflected in the EFC also but was 
not agreed to by the Department of Expenditure. However, the problem areas like acute shortage of mental health professionals, 
lack of proposals and cooperation from the state go vernments have been identified and are being adequa tely addressed by 
increasing the PG training capacity in mental healt h, provision of training for general health staff i n mental health and a more 
active monitoring and mentoring system for involvin g the states actively. More regular interventions h ave been planned and 
would be intensified further once the dedicated monitoring mechanism is in place.  
Recommendations/Observations  
15.12  Majority of districts in the country have no t still been covered under District Mental Health P rogramme, which was 
initiated way back in 1996. It has taken almost 15 years for extending the component to only 123 distr icts. With about 500 
districts still remaining outside the reach of this  vital aspect of the Programme, the Committee can o nly conclude that it has 
failed to take off so far. With the Secretary herse lf indicating a further delay, the Committee does n ot have hope of more 
districts being covered under this component and of  the Programme in the near future. The Committee, t herefore, recommends 
to the Department to seriously take all conceivable  measures to cover the 200 Districts under DMHP, as  envisaged in the 
Eleventh Plan period.  
Action Taken  
  For the plan period, expansion of DMHP to new dis tricts has not been recommended in view of shortage of funds as well 
as shortage of manpower for the programme. However, all conceivable measures to extend the programme to 200 more districts 
will be explored by the programme division. Eleventh Plan covered 27 districts and Tenth Plan covered 110 districts, achieving 
more than the target of expansion of DMHP to 100 districts.

16.8   The Committee is very concerned with the per sisting underutilization of funds earmarked under t he Programme. 
The Committee in its earlier reports has been comme nting on this issue and has been recommending to it  to take some 
corrective steps in this regard. The Committee cann ot accept any justification in this regard as it is  a clear indication of poor 
planning and execution as well as monitoring mechanism. The Committee hopes that at least now, the Department would take a 
more pro-active approach in getting all the requisi te approvals without further delay and try to imple ment the revised 
Programme vigorously in the last two financial years with a focused strategy. 
Action Taken  
  As advised by the Committee, this Ministry has be en taking steps to expedite the completion of codal  formalities related 
to requisite approvals for the National Cancer Cont rol Programme so that implementation of the revised  programme in the 
remaining two financial years i.e. 2010-11 and 2011 -12 can be ensured. The revised NCCP strategy is aw aiting necessary 
approval of CCEA. States in which the programme is proposed to be implemented are being selected. The necessary operational 
technical and financial guidelines for the implementation of the programme are also being finalized. 
XVII. NATIONAL TOBACCO CONTROL PROGRAMME (NTCP) 

Recommendations/Observations  
17.2   The Committee is constrained to observe that  in 2007-08 and 2009-10 BE funds had to be revised downwards 
substantially at the RE stage keeping in view the p ace of expenditure under the Programme. Against an allocation of Rs. 40.00 
crore and 30.00 crore in 2007-08 and 2009-10 respectively, an expenditure of only Rs. 13.98 crore and Rs. 16.60 crore has been 
reported. The Committee opines that time and again the Department brings the rhetoric of scarcity of f unds and here it is not 
utilizing the available resources persistently. The  Committee may be informed of the measures taken by  the Department to 
optimally utilize the available resources under this Programme.  
Action Taken  
  The National Tobacco Control Programme was launch ed in 2007-08 as a pilot programme. In the first ye ar (2007-08), 
expenditure incurred under Tobacco Free Initiative was Rs.13.98 crore against RE allocation of Rs.29.0 0 crore. Subsequently, 
in the year 2008-09, actual expenditure was Rs.35.l7 crore against RE allocation of Rs.39.00 crore. Further in the year 2009-10, 
consequent to CCEA approval in January 2010, out of  RE allocation of Rs.17.00 crore, Rs.16.60 crore wa s utilized under the 
National Tobacco Control Programme. 
  A budget allocation of Rs.45.00 crore has been pr ovided under BE 2010-11 under “Tobacco Free Initiatives”. It has been 
decided to carry out the IEC activities under Natio nal Tobacco Control Programme in four phases to uti lize the budget 
allocation of Rs.23.00 crore in the year 2010-11. An amount of Rs.8.00 crore has already been sanctioned so far for running anti 
tobacco campaign through print and electronic Media  in the first phase of campaign during the month of  May-June, 2010. 
Second phase will be initiated after evaluation of the first phase in the month of August-September, 2010. 
  An amount of Rs.14.84 lakhs has been released dur ing the current FY 2010-11 to State Health Society,  Nagaland for 
carrying out NTCP. Further, State and District Toba cco Control Cells are being asked to furnish the ut ilization certificates in 
respect of grants released to them for carrying out  the National Tobacco Control Programme to enable t his Ministry to release 
next instalment of grant to them for the purpose. 
  An amount of Rs.91.00 lakhs has been released dur ing the current FY 2010-11 to IIPS, Mumbai for carr ying out GATS-
India survey and the remaining instalments will be released after the final report is submitted. 
  The process to procure equipment for setting up o f tobacco testing laboratories has been initiated. 
Recommendations/Observations  
17.9   The Committee notes that though the Departme nt has been able to come up with a law for restricting use and sale of 
the Tobacco products, the ground realities have not changed much. The various provisions of the Act are not being implemented 
in letter and spirit even in metro cities in India.  Situation in respect of small towns and rural area s can well be imagined. The 
Department cannot remain a silent spectator on such  vital issues. A proper pro-active strategy should be developed whereby all 
the States are roped in to work sincerely in this regard. 
Action Taken  
  Since the State Governments/ UTs are the main enf orcing agencies for implementing provisions of the Cigarettes and 
Other Tobacco Products (Prohibition of Advertisemen t and Regulation of Trade and Commerce, Production,  Supply and 
Distribution) Act (COTP A), 2003, they have been as ked to ensure strict compliance of the various prov isions of the Act and 
Rules made thereunder. For regular monitoring of th e progress of implementation of the tobacco control  programme, action 
taken reports are sought from the states. 
  Further, Central Level Steering Committees for lo oking into violation of section 5 of the COTP A has  been constituted. 
An Inter-Ministerial task force has been constituted to look at inter-sectoral issues on tobacco control.

20.2   Besides that, programmes on Control of Human  Rabies and Prevention and Control of Flurosis are still being run on 
pilot project basis. So far only utilization of all ocated fund has been very nominal. The Committee th erefore recommends that

11 is put to use for the targeted beneficiaries. 
Action Taken  
  The reply is at Annexure-XV.  
XXI.  MISCELLANEOUS 
Recommendations/Observations  
21.4   The Committee is happy with the introduction  of such a policy tendering monetary award to infor mers providing 
information about the spurious drugs. The Committee  would like to be apprised of the details of the po licy along with any 
achievement made so far under the policy.  
Action Taken  
  Whistle Blower Scheme ––  Whistle Blower Scheme has been devised by the cent ral Government for giving monetary 
rewards to the Whistle Blower who can take risk of providing the information about the perpetrators fo r such crimes. The 
Reward scheme provides for giving handsome rewards to the informers who provides specific information to the designated 
authorities leading to the seizures of spurious, adulterated, misbranded and not of standard quality drugs, cosmetics and medical 
devices. This reward scheme will be applicable to both the informers as well as the officers of the Government and CDSCO. 
  The Additional secretary, Ministry of Health and Family Welfare has issued letters to all Chief Secr etaries of States/UTs 
on 14th October 2009 with the request for the wide publicity of the Scheme. 
  As per the Whistle Blower Policy, the reward of m aximum of upto 20% of the total cost of consignment s seized will be 
payable to the informer / officials which should no t in any case exceed Rs.25 Lakh in each case. 25% o f the amount will be 
payable to the whistle blower at the time of filing  of complaint in the court of law, 25% at the time of disposal of the case in 
favour of the Government and the rest of the amount, that is, 50% when the case is finally decided and the matter is not pending 
in any court of law in the country. The eligibility  of the informer and the quantum of cash rewards wo uld be decided by a 
Committee, which will consist of officials from different departments / offices. To ensure speedy trials of the cases, these cases 
will be filed before the Designated / Special Court s set up for the purposes of drugs related issues a s per the provisions of the 
Drugs and Cosmetics (Amendment) Act, 2008. 
  Drug Controller General (India) along with other officials will be the nodal authority who will inter alia  oversee the 
functioning of the Reward Scheme as proposed herein above. 
  The Zonal and Sub-Zonal officers of the CDSCO wil l act as the nodal officer to whom the whistle blow er/informer can 
provide the information about the manufacture /movement of spurious /adultered drugs. 
  The identity of the whistle blower/informer will be kept secret and will be known only to the Nodal Officers, i.e. 
concerned zonal and sub-zonal officers of the CDSCO , the DCG (1) and the Director General Health Servi ces. It will be the 
responsibility of the concerned officials to keep the details of the whistle blower / informer secret. 
  On receipt of the information from a whistle blow er/informer, the concerned Nodal officers will orga nize immediate and 
systematic investigation in co-ordination with the State Drugs Control Administration to unearth the spurious drugs racket. 
  The details of the investigations will then be fo rwarded by the concerned zonal/sub-zonal officer to  the DCG (I) for the 
consideration of the committee to decide about the merit of the case for reward and the quantum of rew ard to be given to the 
whistle blower / informer. 
  The CDSCO has received so far 27 cases from the d ifferent parts of the country, however in some case s complaints were 
repeated as they were received twice and some of the cases were found to be fictitious, Rest of the cases are under investigation. 

The details of the current status on the Whistle Bl ower Policy as on 30th April 2010 are at Annexure-XVI  for your kind 
perusal. 
  No reward under the scheme has been offered as th e first instalment has to be given after launching of prosecution in the 
court of Law. 
 
 
 
CHAPTER-III 
 
RECOMMENDATIONS/OBSERVATIONS IN RESPECT OF WHICH REPLIES OF THE GOVERNMENT HAVE 
NOT BEEN ACCEPTED BY THE COMMITTEE 
II.   BUDGETARY ALLOCATION 
Recommendations/Observations  
2.7   The Committee is dismayed at the fact that it s continuous concerns and recommendations to the De partment to put a 
proper mechanism in place for - presenting its case for fund requirement before the Planning Commission in a realistic manner, 
and to arrest the trend of under utilization of funds seem to have failed to make any impact. Atleast the Department needs to try 
some innovative methods to achieve optimum utilization of funds. Health is a crucial sector for the allround development of the 
nation and accordingly the Committee has been advoc ating higher allocation of funds for this sector ev ery year. But with the 
Department itself admitting to the slow pace of exp enditure in respect of many schemes/programmes init iated by it, the 
Committee finds advocacy for additional funds servi ng no purpose. The Committee, therefore, recommends  to the Department 
to undertake a comprehensive analysis of the trend of expenditure for the past three years with a view  to identifying the 
underlying causes of underutilization. Such an exer cise should enable the Department to not only impro ve the utilization level 
of the budgeted funds but also to improve upon the existing system of assessing requirement of funds. The Committee is also of 
the opinion that the Department needs to take up wi th the implementing agencies as and when schemes ar e found not being 
implemented as per the Action Plan.  
Action Taken  
  The Department examines critically each proposal before arriving at the requirement of budgetary allocations for the plan. 
The Department assesses the fund requirement based on the several factors like expenditure trends of e ach 
scheme/programme/institution, status of New Schemes - whether EFC/SFC has been prepared or not, finaliz ation of tendering 
process in respect of construction activities, proc urement of Machines and Equipments, etc. The requir ements of outlay are 
conveyed to the Planning Commission for approval. H owever, it is the jurisdiction of the Planning Comm ission to decide the 
plan outlay for the Ministries/Departments based on  the overall resources available and priorities dec ided by the Planning 
Commission. 
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE  
  The Committee had urged upon the Department to unde rtake a comprehensive analysis of the trend of 
expenditure for the past three years with a view to improve the utilization level of the budgeted funds as also to improve 
upon the existing system of assessing requirement o f funds. However, the Committee is constrained to o bserve that the 
Department’s response has been outrightly evasive in as much as it has reproduced its existing procedure of examination 
of funds requirement. The onus of providing Plan Ou tlay has been solely put on the Planning Commission . The 
Committee opines that if the utilisation of funds had been sub-optimal, how it can expect allocation of enhanced amount 
of funds from the Planning Commission for its vario us programmes/policies/schemes. The Committee finds that sincere 
efforts are lacking on the part of the Department i n this direction. No innovative or new strategy, as  exhorted by the 
Committee, seems to have been worked out by it. In such a scenario, the Committee does not hope any positive change in 

this regard if the Department’s approach remains un changed. The Committee, therefore, recommends to th e 
Department to put forth concerted efforts to convince the Planning Commission for allocation of adequate funds.
