III.  CENTRAL GOVERNMENT HEALTH SCHEME (CGHS) 
Recommendations/Observations  
3.6   The Committee is happy to note that a number of initiatives under CGHS are proposed to be undert aken during 2010-11. 
One such initiative is expansion of a pilot project  for Preventive Health Check-up for pensioners and serving employees over 
the age of 40 years at CGHS dispensary, Sector-12 a nd Sector-4, R.K. Puram, New Delhi. The Committee w ould like the 
Department to share its experiences of the pilot pr oject and recommends to the Department to expand th e initiative to more 
dispensaries in a time-bound manner. 
Action Taken  
  Preventive health care check-up for CGHS benefici aries above the age of 40 years was introduced on 2 4th February, 
2010, for a period of six months, as a pilot project in two Wellness Centres (earlier known as Wellness Centres) in Ramakrishna 
Puram with a view to conducting periodic examinatio n which can not only detect disease early but also help in stopping before 
it starts. Such a preventive check up is a health a ppraisal whose scope and timing are tailored to age , risk factors and life style. 
Under this project, the. following tests/investigations are carried out: 
Male Female 
Hb; Blood Sugar (Fasting & PP); ‘LFT; 
KFT; T3 T4 TSH; Lipid Profile and 
Hb; Blood Sugar (Fasting & PP); LFT; 
KFT; T3 T4 TSH; Lipid Profile; ECG 
ECG and PAP Smear 
 
  For carrying out the health check-up of CGHS bene ficiaries, a concessionaire was selected on the bas is of response 
received to the tender floated by the CGHS. For car rying out the tests, the concessionaire would charge Rs.800/- (Rupees Eight 
hundred only) for every male checked and Rs.900/-(R upees Nine hundred only) for every female checked. The cost of health 

check up of pensioner beneficiaries are borne by th e CGHS and the respective Ministries/Departments wo uld reimburse the 
expenditure incurred by serving employees. 
  After investigations, the beneficiaries undergo h istory taking, general examination, eye examination  and gynaecology 
examination. They are then examined by medical specialists. Based on the examinations, the beneficiaries, where necessary, are 
advised for follow-up and further examination and treatment from specialists. The beneficiaries are advised about the following 
four categories: 
/circle6 Apparently healthy (for follow-up after one year);  
/circle6 At risk of diseases (Next check-up after six months); 
/circle6 Require further investigations(advised investigations and follow up after 90 days); and 
/circle6 Found to have diseases (To be referred to specialist). 
  So far 948 beneficiaries have taken advantage of the health check-up facility introduced on 24th February, 2010. No final 
decision has been taken (i) either for the extensio n of the duration of the pilot project; or (ii) for  extending the project to areas 
covered by other wellness centres.  
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE  
  The Committee in its earlier report had appreciated  Department’s initiative of introducing a pilot pro ject for 
Preventive Health Check-up for pensioners and servi ng employees over the age of 40 years at CGHS dispe nsaries, 
Sector-12 and Sector-4, R.K. Puram, New Delhi. As i nformed a good number of beneficiaries have benefit ted from this 
facility since its introduction on 24th February, 2 010. However, contrary to Committee’s recommendatio n, the 
Department is non-committal on either the extension  of the duration of the pilot project or extending the project to the 
areas covered by other wellness centres. The Commit tee would like to reiterate its earlier stand that in view of the 
increased lifestyle related diseases, the Departmen t needs to expand this initiative to expand its out reach to more 
beneficiaries. 
Recommendations/Observations  
3.7   The Committee also welcomes the introduction of a proposal under which the empanelled hospitals will forward the bill 
for the treatment of pensioner CGHS beneficiaries t o UTI-TSL and payment of the same within 10 days. S uch an initiative is 
bound to help the pensioners in availing of healthcare without any hindrance. 
Action Taken  
  Matter of Record. 
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE  
  The Committee, while making the observation in para  3.7 had expected the Department to apprise it of t he 
updated status of the proposal regarding forwarding the Bill for treatment of pensioner CGHS beneficiaries to UTI-TSL 
for payment within 10 days .The Committee, therefor e, once again recommends to be enlightened about th e progress 
made in the matter. 
Recommendations/Observations  
3.9   The Committee views the introduction of the c urrent proposal of the Ministry with serious concern. The Committee is not 
convinced by Department’s categorical statement that it does not intend to replace the CGHS with this new insurance scheme. It 
is amply clear that the scheme would be mandatory f or future employees and pensioners. The Committee w ould like to point 
out that CGHS is one of the very important social s ecurity measures which has been providing health ca re services at 
economical rates to its beneficiaries for so many decades. The Committee fails to comprehend the rationale for depriving all the 
beneficiaries of such an essential service in the l ong run. The Committee would like to point out that  there is a well-structured 
set up of CGHS dispensaries, Government Hospitals p roviding allopathic and AYUSH healthcare and also a  number of 
empanelled diagnostic centres and private hospitals . In the recent years a number of initiatives have also been taken for 

strengthening/revamping different components under the Scheme. A number of additional dispensaries are  also proposed to be 
set up in the near future. The existing CGHS scheme  is part of the social responsibility of government  for its serving/retired 
employees. Full reimbursement as per the prescribed  norms is payable to the beneficiaries. There is no  upper limit on annual 
expenditure on the treatment of a beneficiary. The very idea of capping the amount of coverage to Rs. 5 lakh per family per year 
as well as the limit of coverage to only - Self, sp ouse, two dependent children and dependent parents and thereby forcing for 
payment of the fixed percentage of premium per additional dependent family member under the proposed scheme indicates that 
the Government intends to dilute its social obligation primarily to its employees. This is unacceptable to the Committee. 
Action Taken  
  The proposed health insurance scheme is primarily  aimed at Central Government pensioners residing in areas not covered 
by the CGHS and also for serving Central Government  employees presently not covered by the CGHS. Exist ing CGHS 
beneficiaries will have the option of continuing wi th the CGHS or opt for the proposed health insuranc e scheme, when 
introduced. 
  Additionally, the cost of running the CGHS in the  existing 25 cities has been increasing alarmingly and the Government is 
finding it difficult to fund the increasing need of  funds to maintain the present level of satisfactio n. Presently, CGHS 
beneficiaries are reimbursed their expenditure base d on the package rates fixed for different treatmen t in private hospitals and 
diagnostic centres. Here also, differential entitlement of wards exists. 
  As per information available from IRDA, more than  97% of the claims settled under different mediclaim/health insurance 
policies were claims under Rs.3.00 lakhs; and 0.35%  of the claims were between Rs. 3.00 lakhs and Rs.5 .00 lakhs and the 
balance were over Rs. 5.00 lakhs. Hence, the present proposed coverage of upto Rs. 5.00 lakhs per beneficiary per annum would 
be more than adequate to cover the health needs of the beneficiaries who would be joining the health insurance scheme. 
  It is not that the beneficiaries would be left hi gh and dry if the expenditure exceeded RS.5.00 lakns in any given year. The 
proposal includes a provision for the creation of c orpus for reimbursement of expenditure beyond the a nnual limit of Rs. 5.00 
lakhs, on a case to case basis. What is being propo sed in the scheme is that the liability of the insu rance agency would be upto 
the limit of Rs.5.00 lakhs. 
  In order to encourage small family norms, it is p roposed to restrict the number of persons to be cov ered under the 
proposed scheme. It is denied that the Government i s diluting its social obligation primarily to its e mployees. The proposed 
scheme allows the coverage of self, spouse, two dependent children and dependent parents, which clearly shows that the benefit 
is sought to be extended to the nearest family members of the beneficiary. 
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE  
  The Action Taken Note furnished by the Department h as proved beyond doubt the apprehensions of the 
Committee that the Government wants to dilute its s ocial obligation towards its employees. The justifi cation by the 
Department that the proposed health insurance schem e is primarily aimed at Central Government pensione rs residing 
in the areas not covered by the CGHS and also for t he serving Central Government employees presently not covered by 
the CGHS, does not hold ground as it would be manda tory for the new entrants in Government service apd  those 
retiring subsequent to its introduction. Further, t he justification that the capping is a well thought  out exercise and 
would be more than adequate to cover the health needs of the beneficiaries is not convincing at all. To prove its point the 
Department has even stated that a provision for the  creation of corpus for reimbursement of expenditur e beyond the 
annual limit of Rs.5.00 lakhs, on a case to case ba sis, is also a part of the scheme. The Committee fi nds this very 
intriguing that when it is ready to shell out reimb ursement over and above this limit, why the limit h as been prescribed 
at all for the beneficiaries. It is understandable that the Department needs to place a capping while negotiating with an 
insurance agency, but keeping discretion of Departm ent to reimburse amount over and above this amount on a case to 
case basis dilutes the responsibility of the Depart ment and can lead to favouritism or even corruption . Another major 
concern is that whether the Insurance agency with w hich the Government is proposing to reach a tie up would promote 
cashless facilities with beneficiaries. The Committ ee has serious apprehensions about the adequacy of this scheme. The 
Committee, accordingly, outrightly rejects the forc ed enforcement of the proposed Insurance Scheme to Government 

servants and recommends to the Department to concen trate on strengthening this well structured CGHS sc heme and 
continue to fulfil Government’s social obligation towards its employees. 
Recommendations/Observations  
3.11   The Committee is dismayed at the Department’ s approach from the very outset. First, it had taken the plea of a large 
number of private hospitals and diagnostic centres empanelled under the CGHS and the opposition from t he staff side to any 
such move. Then, subsequent to Committee’s recommen dation that flexible timings are order of the day a nd desirable in the 
interest of many of the CGHS beneficiaries who are unable to avail the facilities of dispensaries in t he morning hours, the 
Department in its Action Taken Note on Committee’s 27th Report had stated that the Ministry intends to take the matter before 
the Committee of Secretaries. The latest reply prov es beyond doubt that the Department is not willing to move forward in the 
matter. Like in the previous instances, the new ple a taken by the Department is its inability to go ah ead on account of its 
existing staff strength. The Committee is of the op inion that if there is shortage of manpower, necess ary action plan may be 
drawn up and approval sought for requisite manpower  at appropriate level. The Committee would like to reiterate that the 
matter needs to be taken up seriously and in the ri ght spirit. The Committee would like to be informed  of the progress made in 
this regard.  
Action Taken  
  It is the experience that only about 25% of the d octors recommended for appointment by the UPSC repo rt for duty. With 
this trend of in-take, CGHS experiences shortfall i n its day-to-day working. Opening of second shift w ill necessarily involve 
shifting of staff from the present morning shift, leading to further shortage of staff. 
  The proposal was internally discussed in the CGHS . The proposal is a pro-beneficiary move to provide  CGHS benefits 
especially to serving employees, but all were of un animous opinion that as per existing staff position , starting of this double 
shift Wellness Centre will further increase the wor kload and shall be taxing on the routine facilities  being provided during the 
day in the Wellness Centers, especially in view of “posting of CGHS doctors on administrative posts an d other duties of 
Emergency nature”. 
  It is pertinent to mention here that appointment of retired doctors on contractual basis was taken b ecause of ever existing 
shortage of GDMOs in CGHS only as small percentage of doctors, who are given appointments by the Minis try actually join. 
Posting of contractual doctors in CGHS, although, h as given some relief, but obviously cannot match fi lling up of the posts by 
regular appointments. 
  It was felt that starting double shift in wellnes s centre is seemingly not feasible. It was agreed t hat the proposal may be 
kept in abeyance till such time the in-position of the various categories of staff improves to the sanctioned strength. 
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE  
  The Committee has been advocating the idea of CGHS being transformed into a truly service oriented ven ture of 
the Department. However, to its dismay, the Committ ee is finding it very discouraging on the part of t he Department 
not taking it in the right spirit and that too when  it is for its own set of employees. The Committee fully shares the 
concerns of the Department regarding shortage of do ctors. The Department needs to be receptive to the idea, which is 
apparently lacking. The Committee would like to poi nt out that when the double shift system was operat ional, morning 
and evening shift timings were having lesser hours.  With the switch-over to double shift system, the p resent duty hours 
would be accordingly adjusted. The question of havi ng substantial additional staff would, therefore, n ot arise. 
Therefore, the Committee would again recommend to t he Department to improve upon the work culture acco rding to 
the need of the time and move forward in this regard. 
Recommendations/Observations

COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE  
  The Committee observes that 13 plots of land in dif ferent localities of NCT of Delhi are under the pos session of 
CGHS for construction of CGHS Wellness Centres. All  these plots of land are at various stages of appro val. Status 
Report submitted by the Department clearly indicates that construction work is not going to start at any of the centre in 
the near future. The Committee is not aware about s ince when this process started. The Committee can o nly conclude 
with conviction that considerable time has passed a nd no progress worth mentioning is expected. The Co mmittee is 
intrigued with the details made available about Wel lness Centre at Vasant Vihar. It is very strange th at although the 
sanction for the Centre is not there, construction of boundary wall and provision of security at the p remises has been 
duly sanctioned. The Committee apprehends that this particular plot at Vasant Vihar has been lying ear marked for the 
past so many years. The Committee recommends that t he Department should review the position in respect  of all these 
plots of land and expedite the construction process of Wellness Centres. 
Recommendations/Observations  

3.28  The Committee would like to point out that pr ior to 2001, all the premier specialized hospitals had opted for CGHS 
empanelment. The Committee wonders as to whether th e recently empanelled hospitals of Delhi can provid e the same services 
as being provided by Rajiv Gandhi Cancer Hospital o r Dharamshila Cancer Hospital. The Committee is als o not aware about 
the quality status of the only empanelled hospital.  The Committee reiterates that the kind of speciali zed services being offered 
by a particular hospital, specially for terminal di seases like cancer and not the lowest rates should be the basic criteria for 
selection of a hospital. One must not forget that t he main objective of CGHS is to provide quality hea lth care at economical 
rates. Not only this, Government is supposed to rei mburse only the packages rates. Payment of differen ce in treatment 
expenditure is the concern of the beneficiary. In C ommittee’s view, package rates need to be governed by quality service. 
Nobody stops the concerned authorities to negotiate  with the applicant hospitals for arriving at mutua lly acceptable package 
rates. It cannot be that services offered by a smal l hospital can be considered on an equal footing wi th an advanced well 
established hospital. The Committee, accordingly, recommends framing of a policy for revision of rates after every two years to 
realistically fix the rates of treatment. The Committee may be informed about the action taken by the Department in this regard.  
Action Taken  
  As per instructions issued by the Central Vigilan ce Commission, negotiation with bidders is forbidde n in the ordinary 
course. In exceptional cases, negotiation may take place about price/rate alone, and that too with the  lowest responsive bidder 
referred as “L-l”. At best, CGHS can offer L-l rate to all the bidders who had participated in the tender process. 
  It has been decided that from now on the package rates offered to empanelled hospitals and diagnosti c centres would be 
valid for a period of two years with option to cont inue the same for one more year with mutual consent , and thereafter, action 
would be initiated for revision of package rates by floating of fresh tenders. 
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE  
  The Committee is happy to note that a decision has been taken for keeping the package rates offered to empanelled 
hospitals and diagnostic centres valid for a period  of two years with the option to continue the same for one more year 
with mutual consent. The Committee would like to re iterate that for life-threatening diseases like can cer, specialized 
hospitals are the best option for patients. In the Capital where the maximum number of CGHS beneficiar ies are 
residing, facilities available at Hospitals like Ra jiv Gandhi Cancer Hospital and Dharamshila Cancer H ospital not 
reaching them due to their remaining outside CGHS empanelment cannot be ignored and needs due consideration of the 
Department. The Committee would, therefore, like to  emphasize that feasibility of such specialized hos pitals. under 
CGHS may be explored.

The Committee however, feels that wide publicity th rough local and print media needs to be given in th is regard. Only then 
CGHS beneficiaries will be in a position to avail of such facilities  
Action Taken

COMMENTS  OF  THE  COMMITTEE

then CGHS beneficiaries will be in a position to avail of such facilities. 
IV.   SAFDARJUNG HOSPITAL AND VARDHMAN MAHAVEER MED ICAL COLLEGE, NEW DELHI 
Recommendations/Observations  

4.4   The Committee is very concerned with the vaca ncy position in different categories of posts at th e Hospital. This coupled 
with the admission of the head of the Hospital itse lf that the sanctioned strength is far below the pr actical requirement, presents 
a sorry state of affairs. The Committee is of the o pinion that vacancies irrespective of any category are not only directly or 
indirectly bound to have an adverse impact on the quantity and quality of the services rendered by the Hospital but also result in 
overstressing of the existing staff. Further, the vacancies also have the potential to affect the quality of education imparted at the 
attached medical college. The Committee is given to  understand that in case of Resident Doctors, vacan cies occur frequently 
and are filled regularly depending on the requireme nt. The Committee is, however, not able to comprehe nd as to why there is 
about 25% of vacancies at any given point of time. At least the Department can take pre-emptive action  so that it has the next 
batch of Resident Doctors available to step-in in p lace of the outgoing Resident Doctors. Similarly, v acancies in Group 'A' and 
Group 'B' Gazetted posts are far above the justifiable limits. The Committee accordingly recommends to the Department to take 
up the issue of vacancies seriously and on priority basis. It also recommends a review of the requirement of manpower vis-a-vis 
the sanctioned strength of the Hospital and college . The Committee would like to be informed about the  progress made in this 
regard. 
Action Taken  
  Vacancy position:  The sanctioned strength of Group 'A' is 383 and out of these 63 are vacant. The sanctioned strength of 
Group 'B' is 56 and out of these 28 are vacant. The  Hospital has taken up various steps with the compe tent authority for filling 
up of these posts. Regular requisitions are also sent to the UPSC for filling up CHS posts in the hospital. 
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE 
  The Committee is dismayed at the way the issue of  manpower is being consistently handled by the Department for 
so long. The Action Taken Note furnished in the ins tant case reflects the lackadaisical attitude of th e Department in the 
matter. The Department has only informed the vacanc y position status as well as the procedure of filli ng up them. 
Action on the Committee’s recommendation regarding review of manpower vis-a-vis  the sanctioned strength of the 
Safdarjung Hospital and Vardhman Mahavir College as  well as pre-emptive action in case of vacancies of  Resident 
Doctors do not find any place in Department’s reply. The Department needs to make sincere efforts and indicate specific 
measures taken to address the issue of manpower whi ch is crucial for better delivery of healthcare ser vices by the 
Hospital which caters to lakhs of poor patients coming from different regions of the country. 
Recommendations/Observations  
4.5   Committee’s attention was also drawn towards acute shortage of nursing staff at the Hospital. No n-availability of good 
accommodation for nursing staff was cited as the ma in factor responsible for such a situation. Existin g staff quarters at 
Srinivaspuri were considered to be too inadequate. The Committee was given to understand that another project was coming up 
at Dwarka. The Committee is of the view that provis ion of accommodation nearest to the Hospital is the  basic requirement for 
all hospital employees, especially for the nurses r endering 24 hour services. The Committee, according ly, recommends that 
provision of required accommodation facilities for nurses should be taken up on priority basis. Viabil ity of such facility at the 
Hospital premises itself under the ongoing Redevelopment Plan of the Hospital needs to be looked into. 
Action Taken  
  Accommodation of Staff Nurse:  The land approx. 8000 sq. mtr. was acquired at Dwarka for construction of 96 Type-III 
and 16 Type-IV residential quarters for Nurses. At present there is no space available in the hospital  premises for construction 
of residential quarters for nurses. However, there is a piece of land approx. 5.86 acres of Safdarjung  Hospital adjacent of 
Incinerator Plant which is under litigation. Another land measuring approximately 16.0 acres behind the Trauma Centre, AIIMS 
has been acquired by the L&DO. In this regard, Ministry of Health and Family Welfare has taken up with the Ministry of Urban 
Development for allotting some of the lands adjacen t to the hospital for further expansion of the hosp ital. If some lands are 
allotted to Safdarjung Hospital the same can also be used for the residential quarters of Nurses of Safdarjung Hospital. 
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE 

  The Committee is happy to note that possibility o f land being available behind the Trauma Centre, AIIMS and the 
area adjacent to Incinerator Plant of Safdarjung Ho spital for construction of residential quarters for  nurses is there. 
The Committee would appreciate if this matter is pu rsued vigorously with the concerned authorities so that the nurses 
of Safdurjung Hospital have the required residential facilities. 
Recommendations/Observations  
4.7   The Committee is unhappy with the pace with w hich things are moving in respect of the Re-develop ment Plan of the 
Hospital. The Committee in its 27th Report had urge d the Department to make wholehearted efforts to ex pedite the procedural 
formalities regarding this. The Committee notes tha t the Department itself has now stated a time line of six years for the entire 
work to complete. Keeping in view the inordinate de lay already made since its inclusion in the Elevent h Plan, the Committee 
recommends to the Department to make all conceivabl e efforts so that the whole redevelopment work is c ompleted within this 
period of six years. For this to happen, targets need to be set, resources placed at the disposal in time and implementing agencies 
need to be made accountable to accomplish the set targets from the very outset. 
Action Taken  
  The proposal for the redevelopment of Safdarjung Hospital is being further considered. 
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE  
  The Committee has been monitoring the Re-developmen t Plan of the Safdarjung Hospital since the outset and its 
observations and recommendations as contained in it s 27th and 39th Reports are indicative of the fact that the 
Department is not addressing the bottlenecks in imp lementing the Re-development Plan. The present stat us furnished 
by the Department also apparently shows no forward movement in this regard. The Committee is constrained to observe 
that this is a classic case of procrastination. The  Department had earlier set a timeline of six years  for completion of the 
project. However, the Department’s reply that the p roposal for the redevelopment is being further cons idered, entails 
further delay, which is indicative of a very sorry state of affairs. The Committee would like to reite rate that the 
Department needs to come up with a focused strategy  in order to achieve the Re-development Plan in a t ime bound 
manner. 
Recommendations/Observations  
4.10   The Committee is extremely disappointed with  the persisting slow progress made by the Departmen t w.r.t.  setting 
up of the Renal Transplantation Unit and IVF Unit. The Committee recommends to the Department to remove all bottlenecks in 
setting up of the two units at the earliest. The Co mmittee would like to have a status note on the sam e inter-alia  containing 
details regarding the original timeline set, reason s for inordinate delay, resultant cost escalation a nd fresh timeline set with 
special efforts taken to meet the timeline this time.  
Action Taken  
The progress of the setting up of Renal Transplanta tion and IVF lab. has been delayed due to unavailab ility of required space 
for these projects. Nevertheless, efforts have been  made to get certain land allotted from the land wh ich is available near the 
AIIMS trauma centre. It is also anticipated that th e required space for such projects would be address ed in the re-development 
plan of the Hospital. 
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE  
  Expressing extreme disappointment over the persisti ng slow progress with regard to setting up of the R enal 
Transplantation Unit and IVF Unit, the Committee ha d called for a status note inter-alia  containing details regarding 
the original timeline set, reasons for inordinate delay, resultant cost escalation and fresh timeline set with special efforts 
taken to meet the timeline this time. The Departmen t’s Action Taken Note in this regard is evasive and  silent on all the 
issues raised above. It merely points out unavailability of land as a reason for delay. To avoid basic issues of delay, it has 
further been stated that the Re-development Plan of the Institute would be able to address the space s pecific issue. With 

the Re-development Plan of the Institute itself hea ding nowhere at present, the Department’s response does not inspire 
much confidence towards achieving the target of setting up of the Renal Transplantation Unit and IVF Unit. 
V.  DR. RAM MANOHAR LOHIA HOSPITAL, NEW DELHI 
Recommendations/Observations  
5.4   The Committee takes note of the fact that ins pite of a lot of developmental work being undertaken by the Hospital, several 
of the items of work like setting up of the Renal T ransplantation Unit have gone beyond the targeted t imeline. Concerned that 
time-overruns are certain to result in cost overruns, the Committee recommends that the Hospital chalk out a time-bound Action 
Plan for such projects. The Committee would like to  point out that the emphasis should not only be on the optimal utilization 
but also on the judicious utilization of scarce fun ds. The Committee at the same time recommends to th e Department to ensure 
that the developmental activities initiated by the Hospital do not get hampered due to scarcity of funds.  
Action Taken  
  Setting up of Renal Transplantation Unit:  The Renal Transplantation Unit is under establishm ent. The Renal ICU and 
Renal Transplantation Ward have been made. Efforts are on to go for Renal Transplantation by October/N ovember, 2010 for 
which a time- bound action plan is being chalked ou t and only after due training of faculty and manpow er, the Renal 
Transplantation will be started. 
  The hospital has constraints of land and space fo r implementation of several projects already in hand and the land already 
allotted to the hospital at ‘G’ point could not be handed over to the hospital by L&DO. The Hospital h as already taken up the 
matter with the L&DO in April 2010. The Ministry ha s taken up the matter with the Ministry of Urban De velopment for 
allotment of additional land nearby the hospital wh ere the projects like centre of Excellence for Ment al Health, General 
Maternity Centre etc. could come up. 
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE 
  The Committee would like to be apprised about the  reasons for land already allotted to the Hospital at ‘G’ point 
being not handed over by L&DO. If need be, this matter may be taken up at the highest level. 
Recommendations/Observations  
5.7   At the outset the Committee places its disple asure over the mismatch in the figures provided to it with respect to vacant 
posts vis-a-vis  ‘sanctioned and in-position’ posts. For example, i n respect of Group 'A' CHS posts, sanctioned and in -position 
posts are 219 and 194 (including 20 contractual pos ts) respectively while vacancies shown are 27. Such  variations exist in 
respect of some other categories of posts also. The  Committee would like the Department to explain thi s mismatch. The 
Committee is constrained to find a lot of vacancies  across the grades. The Hospital is already overbur dened with patient load 
and in such a scenario it is really a matter of con cern that such a large number of vacancies are allo wed to continue. The 
Committee is of the opinion that in the smooth func tioning of the hospital services, all the posts whe ther they are in technical 
side, nursing side or in the clerical side, have th eir part to play. Vacancies beyond permissible limi ts in any grade affect the 
overall patient care offered by the Hospital as well as result in overburdening the existing set of employees. Taking into account 
the increased patient load, the Committee recommend s to the Department to take immediate steps for rea ssessment of the 
manpower requirement in the Hospital and fill up th e vacant posts in the various grades at the earlies t. The Committee may be 
apprised of the steps initiated by the Department t o streamline the existing procedure of recruitment so that vacancies in the 
different grades are filled up at the earliest. 
Action Taken

below. It may be mentioned that the 27 vacancies in  respect of Group 'A' CHS Post have occurred due to  7 newly created post 
for General Maternity Service. The hospital had con ducted Walk-in-Interview and offers issued for cont ractual appointment. 
However, many of the selected candidates have not joined.

Nursing, the filling of which is pending as the proposal for issue of notification of Recruitment Rules is under process. Many of 
the Administrative posts are unfilled for several y ears for which proposals are being initiated for re vival/notification of 
Recruitment Rules. The Group ‘B’ posts are also vacant for similar reasons. As regard Group-'C' posts, there are about 40 posts 
of LDC lying unfilled due to ongoing recruitment pr ocess through SSC. The posts of Stenographers Cadre  are lying vacant for 
want of eligible candidates in the feeder grade and  efforts to fill up these posts through deputation failed despite 
circulation/advertisement twice due to non--response. The remaining promotional posts are being filled through DPC for which 
process is going on. The Group-'C' posts (Nursing Cadre) are being filled through the ongoing recruitment exercise. The written 
examination has already been conducted in May 2010.  Another process of recruitment has also been initi ated for filling newly 
created 119 posts of Staff Nurse. The process of fi lling up of the Technical Group-'C' Posts (Includin g erstwhile Group 'D'

being made to fill the posts of Group ‘C’ and ‘D’ o n contract basis so as to facilitate smooth running  of patient care services at 
the hospital.

knowledge update and providing better services to the patient care/ Hospital establishment.

VI.  ALL INDIA INSTITUTE OF MDICAL SCIENCES (AIIMS)  
Recommendations/Observations  
6.5   The Committee is anguished to note that out o f a total allocation of Rs. 1461 crore for the Eleventh Plan, the Institute does 
not seem to be able to utilize the earmarked funds for the Plan period during the first three years of the Plan period. The Institute 
has been able to utilize only Rs. 648.00 crore (as on 28-2-2010). The Committee sees little hope for o ptimum utilization of 
remaining funds during the last two years of Eleven th Plan. As a result, the initiatives/ongoing proje cts are not likely to be 
executed as targeted originally. The Committee observes that as many as 21 projects were envisaged to be completed during the 
Eleventh Plan for which an outlay of Rs. 623.63 crore was made. The Committee is, however, dismayed to note that by the end 
of the first three years of the Plan, utilization reported is only Rs. 70.18 crore. The situation is worsened by the fact that work in 
respect of as many as 14 projects is yet to start. These projects include expansion of OPD Block and N ew Emergency Ward, 
Burns Unit at JPN Apex Trauma Centre, Multi-level p arking, Pediatrics Surgery. Out of total expenditur e of Rs. 70.18 crore, 
Rs. 55.00 crore has been utilized for minor works i n Hospital/ hostels/ residential areas o(the campus . Thus, progress made in 
the remaining six projects can also be considered a t the initial stage only. The Committee cannot rema in a silent spectator to 
present circumstances as AIIMS is India’s premier I nstitute providing specialized health services to p atients from across the 
country. The Committee is of the firm view that it is high time that an effective monitoring mechanism is set in motion. 
Action Taken 
  An amount of Rs.1461.00 crore has been allocated for the Eleventh Five Year Plan Period. Due to non- finalisation of 
EFCs of various projects, the Institute has been ab le to utilize only Rs.640.74 crore by 31st March, 2 010. The details of 
allocation and Expenditure thereof during the Eleventh Five Year Plan period is given below:-

COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE 
  The Committee is deeply disturbed by the manner i n which the Department is treating its top most Institute of the 
country, more so when there is no scarcity of earma rked funds for its developmental activities. The wo rk and patient 
load of the Institute is increasing alarmingly day by day warranting urgent addition/strengthening of its existing 
infrastructure. There are projects duly approved by the Governing Body on 13th August, 2008, with EFCs submitted or 
to be submitted but work is yet to commence. Either  procedural formalities are still remaining or ther e is a change in 
the location or Master Plan is yet to be approved. The Committee is very surprised to note that even v ery small works 
like additional lift in hospital, expansion of exis ting cafeteria in Eastern campus, construction of c ommunity centre are 
not proceeding further. Though setting up of a Plan ning and Monitoring Committee under the Chairperson ship of 

Secretary, Department of Health and Family Welfare is a welcome step but no concrete steps or strategy  for putting 
things right on track in the next two financial yea rs, has been shared with the Committee. In such a s cenario, the 
Committee can only hope that the Department takes s ome concrete steps to put the developmental agenda of the 
Institute back on track.

Department in co-ordination with AIIMS and all the implementing agencies should carry out the implemen tation of 
these two ambitious projects in a time-bound manner.

in the matter. ‘ Avoiding duplication’ can never be  considered as a valid justification for dropping t his project. The Committee 
notes that funds to the tune of Rs. 48 crore have b een earmarked for this purpose during the Eleventh Plan. The Committee is 
unaware whether the Department, before taking a view in the matter, has taken into account the patient load in the Burns Unit at 
the Safdarjung Hospital. Further, the Committee wou ld also like to point out that availability of any service at Safdarjung 
Hospital cannot be made the criteria for not initia ting the same at AIIMS. If that be so, the same arg ument can be put forth for 
not adding a particular service at Safdarjung Hospi tal since the same was available at the next door A IIMS. The Committee 
would like to point out that with the ever increasi ng patient load, both the hospitals have been provi ding exemplary services. 
Any addition or expansion of service will be going to provide better healthcare to our people. 
Action Taken  
  As desired by the Committee, the matter will be t aken up with the concerned authorities. 
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE 
  Response of the Department about the proposal for  setting up of a Burns Unit at AIIMS does not give much hope 
to the Committee. The Committee would once again reiterate that all efforts need to be made to revive the proposal and 
expedite the setting up of Burns Unit. It would go a long way in reducing the ever increasing patient load of burn victims 
on Safdurjung Hospital. 
Recommendations/Observations  
6.16   The Committee is pained by the acute shortag e of the manpower at the Institute and is of the op inion that the same 
is not justifiable in any manner. The Committee app lauds the existing set of employees of the Institut e, who inspite of the 
tremendous work pressure, have been able to uphold the high standards and reputation of the Institute.  With large number of 
vacancies at Faculty level posts, it is practically  difficult to maintain the high standards of patien t care and teaching in the 
Institute for which it has been known. The Committee accordingly recommends to the Department to make all out efforts to fill 
up the vacant posts - both faculty and non-faculty and all other groups in a time-bound manner.  
Action Taken  
  It is stated that out of 629 sanctioned faculty p osts, 195 posts in various categories are lying vac ant. The main reason 
behind these vacancies is non- finalisation of rese rvation roster. With a view to overcome this situat ion, a former Director, 
DOPT was engaged as Consultant for preparing reservation roster for faculty posts who has submitted his report and the same is 
to be considered by the Institute Body in its next meeting. The reservation roster prepared by Consultant in conformity with the 
DOPT instructions/guidelines is presently under submission to the DOPT. The response of DOPT is awaite d. All efforts would 
be made to fill the vacant faculty posts at the earliest.

  The Committee has been constantly monitoring the i ssue of vacancies in AIIMS, particularly the vacanc ies at the 
Faculty level posts, subsequent to the matter being  brought before it in November 2008 by the Faculty Association of 
AIIMS through a representation. The Committee is aware that the matter of reservation has been amicabl y resolved by 
the Department after intervention of the PMO and se tting up of a high level Committee in this regard. But the 
Committee is upset over the delay in implementing the final outcome as no forward movement seems to have been made 

since the Secretary, Health and Family Welfare’s pr esentation before the Committee during examination of Demands 
for Grants 2010-11 in March, 2010. The Committee, therefore, urges the Department to take up the issue of filling up of 
these Faculty level posts with a sense of urgency s o that this issue gets sorted out without further l oss of time. The 
Committee feels that now that the reservation roste r has been prepared by the Consultant, the Departme nt should 
vigorously pursue the matter with DoPT for prompt response. 
  As regards vacancies in other categories of posts , the Committee is aware that the patient load of t he Institute is 
increasing day by day as the Institute caters to th e needs of poor and needy patients from the remotes t corners of the 
country in the absence of the quality tertiary heal thcare there. The Department needs to not only fill  up the existing 
vacancies urgently, but also needs to reassess its manpower requirements regularly. The Department’s r esponse in this 
regard is silent on the, issue of vacancies. The Co mmittee would like to be apprised of the specific s teps taken by the 
Department in this regard. 
Recommendations/Observations  
6.19   The Committee would also like to invite the attention of the Department to its concerns raised in its 27th Report on 
Demands for Grants of the Department wherein it has  cited a WHO report according to which out of 428 s tudents of AIIMS 
(studying between 1989 and 2000), 233 (54%) are res iding abroad. This presents a very disturbing pictu re that on the one hand 
there is acute shortage of staff in the Institute and on the other hand pass outs from this one of the best training grounds in India 
are turning to greener pastures, that too after ava iling highly subsidized quality education. The Comm ittee therefore reiterates 
it’s earlier stand that the Department should serio usly come up with some kind of bond or legislation in respect of premier 
institutes like AIIMS so that the young graduates o r post graduates are made to serve in AIIMS or othe r Government 
Hospitals/Institutes for a certain period. The Committee wishes to be informed of some concrete steps in this regard.  
Action Taken  
  It may be stated that during the year 1989 to 200 0 about 565 MBBS students have passed from the Inst itute. There is no 
reservation for AIIMS medical graduates (MBBS) in t he Postgraduate seats viz.  MD/MS in AIIMS, therefore they left the 
Institute. After that, the AIIMS has no information of their whereabouts. Hence it cannot be confirmed or denied that out of 565 
MBBS graduates from this Institute, 233 MBBS graduates are residing abroad. It is also mentioned that there is no Bond system 
for retaining these pass out students at AIIMS. However, as desired by the Committee, the possibility of evolving such a system 
will be explored. 
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE 
  The Committee is very surprised by the response o f the Department on a very pertinent issue. It is v ery strange 
that a premier Institute like AIIMS does not mainta in any record about its passouts, that too in this era of high tech. 
connectivity. The Committee strongly feels that whe n an international organization like WHO can conduc t an 
assessment in this regard, similar step can be easi ly initiated by AIIMS also. The Committee would lik e to be informed 
about the steps taken by the Department so as to ev olve a mechanism/ procedure for utilizing the services of its students 
on completion of their studies.

XI.  COMMON RECOMMENDATIONS REGARDING HOSPITALS 
Recommendations/Observations  
11.1   The Committee is concerned with the procedur al hassles earning in the way of the developmental work in various 
hospitals and institutions of repute under the Depa rtment. Though the Committee considers the laid daw n procedures as means 
of systemic checks and balances, yet it finds no merit in their coming in the way of urgent developmental work, and that too far 
a project targeted at taking care of the health care needs of the common man of the country. The Committee is of the considered 
opinion that the imperatives of development and env ironmental concerns need to be reconciled without f urther less of time to 
avoid further time overruns in respect of this proj ect. The Committee therefore recommends to the Depa rtment to have a 
centralized cell under a dedicated senior officer t o coordinate all such procedural issues with the va rious agencies. The 
Department needs to. playa proactive role here. If required, the matter may be taken up at the senior most level to facilitate early 
sanction of requisite statutory clearances and the concerned approving authorities may also be apprise d of the Committee’s 
concern aver approval delays in this regard. The Committee may be informed about the progress made in this regard.  
Action Taken  
  As of now, there is no such proposal to set up a centralized cell. However, developmental works pertaining to Safdarjung 
Hospital and Dr. RML Hospital, New Delhi have been closely monitored appropriately by the concerned Senior Officers. 
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE 
  Perturbed over the persistent delay caused in coo rdinating the procedural issues with various agenci es of the 
Centre and the State Governments in respect of several developmental initiatives of the Department, the Committee had 
urged the Department to have a positive approach an d accordingly mooted the idea of having a centraliz ed cell under a 
dedicated senior officer to coordinate all such pro cedural issues with the various agencies in respect  of all the 

Government run hospitals of India. While making the  above recommendation, the Committee’s only concern  was that 
procedural delays should not be allowed to put a sp anner in the works of developmental plans of hospit als and 
institutions. The Committee is in no way interested  in suggesting the nitty-gritty of the monitoring f ramework, but 
would like to see the progress on the ground. Howev er, the Committee is at a loss to understand the gr ounds of the 
Department’s reluctance in having a centralized cell for coordination and would like to be enlightened about it. 
Recommendations/Observations  
11.2   The Committee is pained by the fact that the  healthcare fraternity under the Department has to function under very 
stressful conditions. Taking strong view of the eno rmous patient load in almost all the institutions/h ospitals under the 
Department, which is in many cases manifold than en visaged, the Committee would like to make a general  recommendation to 
the Department. It recommends strongly to the Depar tment to have an independent reassessment of manpow er done in all the 
institutions/hospitals under it - sanctioned vis-a-vis  the patient load of the hospital. The Committee al so recommends a close 
monitoring of the vacancies in all the institutions /hospitals of the Department under a dedicated seni or officer for coordination 
between the institutions/hospitals and the recruitment agencies so as to facilitate early filling up of vacant posts. 
Action Taken  
  In so far as Safdarjung Hospital and Dr. RML Hosp ital, New Delhi is concerned, review meetings to ad dress the pending 
issues including filling up of manpower are constan tly held in the Ministry. Appropriate steps are tak en to fill up the vacancies 
arising from time to time.  
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE  
  The Committee is dismayed at the manner in which th e Department has attempted to skirt the issue of ma king an 
independent assessment of manpower requirement in h ealth institutions. The Committee had heard the hea ds of many 
hospitals based in Delhi during its examination of Demands for Grants of the Department in March 2010 and one of the 
glaring problems put forth by them was that of the manpower crunch. The Committee is also aware of the  fact that 
manpower requirements of almost all the hospitals h as not been analyzed for many years inspite of the patient and 
workload increasing manifold, thereby warranting corresponding increase in staff strength. Accordingly, the Committee 
had urged upon the Department to have a dedicated o fficer assigned the task of taking care of the vaca ncies and 
workload specific issues at the central level and c oordinate with the recruiting agencies as well. How ever, the 
Department has evaded an elaborate reply and merely  made a generic statement regarding existing proced ure w.r.t.  the 
Safdarjung Hospital and Dr. RML Hospital. The Commi ttee wonders as to what could be the reason for so many posts 
lying vacant for so many years inspite of constant holding of review meetings to address the pending i ssues including 
filling up of manpower w.r.t.  these two hospitals. The Department needs to welco me fresh and innovative ideas to 
smoothen and facilitate processes rather than adopting an indifferent attitude. 
XIII. PRADHAN MANTRI SWASTHYA SURAKSHA YOJANA (PMSSY) 
Recommendations/Observations  
13.19  The Committee is happy to note that the second phase will extend the reach of AIIMS like facilities to more underserved 
areas of the country and will help in upgradation o f other medical college institutions. However, the Committee cautions the 
Department to be careful from the outset so that th e problems that afflicted the first phase do not re cur here. The Committee 
would like to be apprised of the progress made in implementing the second phase of the Scheme.  
Action Taken  
  Both in West Bengal and Uttar Pradesh, a final de cision is to be taken on the location of the propos ed facility. 
Concurrence of the State Government on the availability of land at the location chosen would also need to be obtained. 
  As regards 6 upgradation projects in the second p hase of PMSSY, tender document for civil work at Government Medical 
College, Amritsar has been finalized and is being f loated shortly. Government Medical College, Nagpur has been released 

Rs.40 crore for procurement of equipments. Concept plans of the four remaining institutions are being prepared by the Project 
Consultants.  
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE  
  The Committee recommends to the Department to proac tively take up the matter with the concerned State 
Governments of West Bengal and U.P. so as to achiev e the setting up of the AIIMS like institutes there , within targeted 
timelines. 
 
 
XIV.   DEVELOPMENT OF NURSING SERVICES  
Recommendations/Observations  
14.8   Another area of concern for the Committee ha s been strengthening of RAK College of Nursing as C entre of 
Excellence. Even before the beginning of the Eleventh Plan, this project is being shown as under progress. The Committee finds 
that the work for upgradation of RAK College of Nur sing into Centre of Excellence had been entrusted t o HSCC which has 
completed the work of renovation of existing buildi ng. However, the new construction has not yet start ed due to pending 
clearances and approval of concept plan from various statutory bodies. When asked on this specific iss ue during the meeting of 
the Committee on 23rd March, 2010, the Committee wa s informed that there has been a problem due to the  fact that the 
Department does not have the ownership of the land in favour of RAK College of Nursing. The matter has  been taken up with 
the Department of Urban Development and the Land and Development Office and would be sorted out.  
Action Taken  
  The matter is being reviewed with the HSCC and RA K College of Nursing in the meetings being held at regular intervals.  
Recommendations/Observations  
14.9  The Committee is not happy with the state of affairs of the work of upgradation of RAK college of Nursing into Centre of 
Excellence. This is the position when this old inst itution is located in Delhi itself. The Committee w onders as to whether any 
attempt of monitoring has been made by the Departme nt so far. The project has been in the pipeline for  many years now. The 
Committee recommends to the Department to make sinc ere efforts to sort out all the outstanding issues and problem areas 
hampering in the upgradation of the college at the earliest.  
Action Taken  
  Noted for compliance. Concerted efforts are being  made to sort out the outstanding issues at the earliest. 
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE 
  The Action Taken Note of the Department does not present a very encouraging picture w.r.t. upgradation of RAK 
College of Nursing into Centre of Excellence which has been pending since before the beginning of the Eleventh Plan. 
The Committee neither finds any forward movement on  the issue, nor any commitment, from the Department  in this 
regard. The Department’s efforts are limited to reviewing the matter with HSCC and the college authorities on a regular 
basis. Department’s initiatives to sort out the bot tlenecks and specific problem areas are apparently lacking. Such a 
situation does not inspire any hope for early compl etion of the upgradation work. The Committee would recommend to 
the Department to take some concrete and decisive steps in this regard. 
Recommendations/Observations  
14.12 The Committee appreciates and welcomes the af oresaid initiatives taken by the Department. The Co mmittee infers that 
these measures will help in producing more number o f nursing personnel. The Committee is happy to note  that the Department 
has constructed 413 dwelling units for nurses and t he Directorate of Estate has also allowed accommoda tion for Nurses in 

general pool. However, the Committee is still skeptical whether this would suffice the requirements. The Committee would like 
to be enlightened about the availability of dwelling units vis-a-vis  the demand.  
Action Taken  
  The demand and availability of residential accomm odation for nurses is being assessed in consultation with the concerned 
hospitals. 
  As regards improvement in quality of nursing serv ices in the interest of quality patient care vis-a-vis  fulfilling the 
requirement of nurses by outsourcing, the matter would be taken up with the concerned hospitals. 
 
 
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE 
  The Committee is happy to note that the Departmen t has started consultations with the concerned hosp itals to 
assess their demand and availability of residential  accommodation for nurses. The Committee also appre ciates 
Department’s willingness to take up the matter with the concerned hospitals to fill up the required nu mber of nurses by 
outsourcing. But the Committee cautions the Departm ent to be careful in this regard as outsourcing can  never be a 
solution or substitute to regular employment. The C ommittee would like the Department to apprise the C ommittee 
regarding its outcome on the accommodation assessment exercise as well as the manpower outsourcing aspect. 
XV.  NATIONAL MENTAL HEALTH PROGRAMME (NMHP) 
Recommendations/Observations  
15.6   The Committee is aware that the slow pace of  the implementation during the Tenth Plan has alrea dy resulted in the 
spillover of many components of the Programme to th e Eleventh Plan. The Committee is dismayed at the p ersistent 
discouraging level of achievements under the parame ters, viz. , the financial and physical, set under this ambiti ous Programme. 
The Committee has been voicing its concern time and  again in this regard and recommending to the Depar tment to take 
immediate corrective measures to achieve the target s set under the Programme. But no corrective measur e seems to have been 
taken by the Department so far.  
Action Taken  
  With a dedicated monitoring mechanism approved by  the competent authority and soon to be initiated, the situation of 
lack of proposals and cooperation from States will improve with more proactive role from the centre. A cute shortage of 
manpower which is the other hindrance of the programme is being addressed by implementing manpower development schemes 
on mid to long term and use of trained manpower in the DMHP team to address the acute shortage in short term. 
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE 
  The Committee hopes that with the Department clai ming a “dedicated monitoring mechanism” as well as 
addressing the acute shortage of manpower in short term by “implementing manpower development schemes on mid to 
long term and use of trained manpower in the DMHP team”, things will move in the right direction and bring one of the 
most neglected National Programme back on track. Th e Committee would like to have the details of the “ dedicated 
monitoring mechanism” and the details of addressing  manpower shortage problem for this Programme, put into force 
by the Department.  
Recommendations/Observations  
15.9   As regards, the issue of shortage of manpowe r like trained Psychiatrists, Clinical Psychologist s and Psychiatric 
social workers, the Committee shares the concerns o f the Department. The Committee can only suggest th at the requisite 
manpower needs to be attracted by offering a suitab le compensation package and offering good working c onditions. Another 

initiative which can be taken is to increase the nu mber of seats in the field of Mental Health in the medical colleges across the 
country. The Committee would like to be briefed about the steps taken by the Department in this regard. 
Action Taken  
  The remuneration of mental health professionals h ave been revised in the programme and with effectiv e monitoring 
mechanism soon to be in place, the responsiveness a nd working conditions in the programme is set to im prove. The number of 
PG seats in mental health specialities of Psychiatry, Clinical Psychology, Psychiatric Social Work, Psychiatric Nursing is being 
increased in medical colleges as well as in mental hospitals under the Manpower Development Schemes of  NMHP. The 
manpower development schemes are expected to genera te an estimated no. of 1756 professionals per year in addition to the 
current capacity after implementation of the scheme in 11 centres of excellence and 120 PG departments in mental health during 
the plan period. 
 
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE 
  The Committee is happy to note that some concrete  steps have been initiated by the Department for much-awaited 
revamping and augmentation of the National Mental H ealth Programme. These initiatives need to be const antly 
monitored by the Department in coordination with the implementing agencies. 
Recommendations/observations  
15.11   The Committee notes with displeasure that t hough the Department has proposed to include 200 mo re districts 
under this component during the Eleventh Plan, fina l approval is yet to be given by the competent auth ority. The Committee is 
informed that during 2010-11, the Department proposes to implement DMHP on new pattern to only existing 123 districts. 
Action Taken  
  The department had proposed for expansion of DMHP  to 200 new districts. However, the EFC did not agr ee to the 
proposal of the department in view of paucity of funds.  
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE 
  The main objective of the Programme has been to e nsure the availability and accessibility of minimum  mental 
healthcare for those suffering with mental illness,  particularly to the most vulnerable and underprivi leged sections 
amongst them. The Department would be failing in it s duty if it does not expand the Programme in a big  way so as to 
reach the remotest corners of the country. The Depa rtment cannot shy away from this social responsibil ity. Funds for 
the Programme have been ceremoniously revised downw ards at the RE stage owing to poor implementation s tatus. 
Department’s reply that the EFC did not agree to its proposal for expansion of DMHP to 200 new districts in view of the 
paucity of funds does not sound logical. The Department appears to have not presented its case based on sound logic and 
facts or it might not have inspired confidence of a ctually being able to execute the expansion work ba sed on its track-
record. The Committee, therefore, recommends to the  Department to focus on better utilization of the a llocated funds 
and effective implementation of the Programme. The Committee is sanguine that more efficient use of th e allocated 
funds would bolster the Department’s proposal for expansion of DMHP.  
XVI. NATIONAL CANCER CONTROL PROGRAMME (NCCP)  
Recommendations/observations  
16.3   The Committee has been witnessing a consistent unsatisfactory level of performance under the various components of the 
Programme. Gap in physical targets and achievement level under different components of the Programme i s evident in the 
Elevenrh Plan period also. Neither the existing Reg ional Cancer Centres are being strengthened nor rec ognition being given to 
new Regional Centres. Shortfall is reported in the assistance being provided to existing/new oncology wings of Govt. Medical 
Colleges. District Cancer Programme has also not been showing satisfactory progress. 
Action Taken  

  During the Eleventh Five Year Period, the revised  NCCP was under consideration for which there was s hortfall in the 
assistance provided to existing/new oncology wings of Govt. Medical Colleges and the District Cancer Control Programme. 
Recommendations/observations  
16.4   Performance level of the Programme becomes a ll the more discouraging when it is noticed that th ere is no shortage 
of funds. Funds are being duly allocated every year , religiously reduced at the RE stage and the utili zation status at the end of 
the year reported to be under-utilized. The Committ ee notes the following figures of expenditure for 2 007-08,2008-09 and 
2009-10 in respect of the National Cancer Control Programme (NCCP):-

 2007-08   2008-09       2009-10 (as on 28.2.10)

Action Taken  
  Grant-in-aid could not be released to some of the  Government Medical Colleges/Institutions due to a number of reasons-
viz.  absence of BARC approval and recommendation of the  State Govt. for continuation of the scheme after c essation of the 
Central Govt. assistance. 
  Many of the institutes that had availed grant-in- aid had pending utilization certificates in the abs ence of which further 
grant-in-aid could not be released as per GFR guide lines. The expenditure for 2007-08, 2008-09 and 2009-10 as on 31st March 
in respect of the NCCP was Rs.52.28 crore, Rs.109.00 crore and Rs.40.42 crore respectively.  
Recommendations/observations  
16.5 The Committee is dismayed at the fact that the  Department does not appear to be serious w.r.t. th e implementation of this 
programme and the same is evident from the fact that the EFC for Eleventh Plan is being prepared by th e Department and there 
is no approved scheme for the current Plan. 
Action Taken  
  The Task Force of the NCCP was constituted in 200 5 and its report was received in 2006. Thereafter, the EFC for the 
revised NCCP for the remaining two years of the Ele venth plan was under preparation and the completion  of formalities has 
been undertaken after extensive consultations with stake holders including Planning Commission, Depart ment of Expenditure, 
State Governments/UTs etc. 
Recommendations/observations  
16.6  The Department itself has been admitting canc er to be one of the ten leading causes of death in India with nearly 7 to 9 
lakh cases occurring every year. The Committee opin es that this Programme can be singled out as one of  the classic examples 
where inspite of ample fund allocation and good obj ectives, the Programme has not been able to take of f in the desired manner 
due to apparent apathy of the Department. 
Action Taken  
  The Revision of NCCP for the Eleventh Five Year P lan has taken more time than envisaged involving co nsultations with 
experts, stake holders, State Governments/UTs.  

COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE  
  Department’s response in the Action Taken Note is a  mere acknowledgement of the failures on account of  its 
exercise of revising the NCCP eating into most of t he time during the Eleventh Plan period. Though the  Committee 
acknowledges problems like withholding of grants to  the institutes in accordance with the GFR guidelin es, yet the same 
cannot be termed as the primary or sole reason for poor performance of the Programme. As pointed out earlier also, the 
fact that the Programme is still not having any app roved scheme during the current Plan is proof enoug h of 
Department’s consistent neglect of this Programme. The Department needs to seriously review its implem entation 
strategy which has been hampering implementation of  such a crucial Programme of vital importance for s o long and 
that too when there is no shortage of earmarked funds for this purpose.  
 
 
Recommendations/Observations  
16.7  The Committee is aware that the Department co uld utilize only Rs.252.95 crore during the Tenth Plan out of an approved 
outlay of Rs.333 crore. The Committee notes that th e approved outlay for the Eleventh Plan has been in creased about eight 
times to Rs.2400 crore out of which only Rs.94.16 crore have been utilized upto 28th February, 2010. This leaves Rs.2300 crore 
(approx.) remaining to be utilized during the remai ning two financial years of the Plan. Taking into a ccount the provision of 
Rs.155 crore during 2010-11, it still leaves behind a huge amount of funds to the tune of Rs.2150 crore (approx.) left for the last 
financial year. With the current pace of utilizatio n, the Committee apprehends that it is going to sho w a worse performance 
when compared with the Tenth Plan track record.  
Action Taken  
  The budget allocated for the NCCP in the Eleventh  Five Year Plan was Rs.2400.00 crores. The amount u tilized in the 
Eleventh Five Year Plan till date (31.03.10) is Rs. 201.70 crores. Due to budgetary constraints, the pr oposed budget of NCCP 
has been scaled down to Rs.731.52 crore for the rem aining two years of the Eleventh Five Year Plan. Th e Ministry has also 
initiated a concerted plan of action to implement the revised programme on a priority basis.  
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE  
  The Committee notes that the Department has cited b udgetary constraints as the reason for scaling down  budget 
of NCCP to Rs.731.52 crore for the remaining two ye ars of the Eleventh Plan. The Committee hopes that this 
curtailment is not as a result of sub-optimal utili zation of the allocated funds due to poor implement ation of the 
Programme and wonders as to whether the Department has drawn an effective strategy to utilise even thi s curtailed 
amount fully in the remaining two years. The Commit tee would like to be apprised of the status and com ponents of the 
revised Programme and the details of the “concerted  plan of action to implement the revised programme on a priority 
basis”. 
Recommendations/Observations  
16.9   In order to identify weak links and ensure e ffective implementation of various components of th e programme, the 
Committee had in its 27th Report recommended to the  Department to give utmost priority to evaluation o f the National Cancer 
Control Programme by a premier agency having experience of work in such Disease Control Programmes. Ho wever, in respect 
of a query in this regard, the Committee is informe d that the programme was last evaluated in 2004 by the National Institute of 
Health and Family Welfare (NIHFW). Certain amendmen ts in the programme were incorporated in 2005, in t he light of that 
assessment. Another review has now become due. The process to select an independent outside agency has been initiated. 
16.10   The Committee is unhappy to note that the D epartment has actually not taken any step further in the last two years. 
The Committee would like to point out here that it was informed during examination of the Demands for Grants 2008-09 of the 
Department that the proposal submitted by the NIHFW  for second evaluation of the NCCP was not up to th e mark. In addition 
ICMR also submitted the proposal for evaluating the NCCP. It was stated that the Department decided to  invite proposals from 
other organizations including IIM. However, no resp onse had been received in the matter and hence no f inal decision could be 

taken regarding selection of the agency for evaluat ing the NCCP. The Committee opines that the severe underutilization of 
funds as well as the physical targets under the Pro gramme warrant an immediate evaluation and necessary corrective measures. 
The Committee therefore recommends to the Department to immediately evaluate the implementation of the Programme so that 
all the loopholes and hurdles afflicting the smooth  implementation of such an ambitious Programme are identified and 
corresponding corrective measures taken at the earliest. 
Action Taken  
  The last evaluation of NCCP was carried out in 20 04. One of the premier institutions is under Ministry of Health & FW is 
being short listed to carry out the evaluation as per recommendation of the Committee. 
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE 
  The Committee has been voicing its concern over t he implementation of the Programme since long and 
accordingly had advocated its evaluation in order t o identify weak links and ensure effective implemen tation of its 
various components but no forward movement seems to  have been made in this regard. The Committee is di smayed at 
the fact that even after two and a half years the D epartment is still in search of an agency to take u p this task. The 
Department needs to take this in the right earnest and have the Programme scrutinised by a credible ag ency at the 
earliest. 
Recommendations/Observations  
16.11 The Committee notes that a large number of Utilization Certificates are pending against various colleges spread across the 
country. Maximum amount of pending UCs is against the following colleges:-
